Why Daniel Radcliffe May Not Watch HBO's Harry Potter TV Series

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Will Daniel Radcliffe watch the new Harry Potter TV show on HBO with his family? It seems like he'll be skipping it.

"I don't know if I will [watch], honestly," Radcliffe told the Associated Press (via Deadline). "I've watched, I've been in a lot of Harry Potter already ... and also I don't watch any TV."

That latter claim seems unlikely when you consider that Radcliffe has appeared in a handful of really good TV shows, including one that's running now and happens to be one of the best comedy shows of the year. He brought receipts for this claim, though. "I haven't seen 'Breaking Bad.' I haven't seen 'Mad Men.' I haven't seen some of the like the real classics. So, you know, eight hours is a lot of anything to watch."

Let's be fair: If Radcliffe is being truthful here, he does have some important homework to do. (If he really hasn't watched any television, I'd add some other picks to his queue like "Veep," "Succession," "Vanderpump Rules," and "Fleabag," just to cover a few vital bases.) This also does line up with Radcliffe's apparent attempts, over the years, to distance himself from the wizarding world that made him famous; for those who may not know, the actor played the titular Harry Potter in the original film franchise. In February of 2026, Radcliffe told Parade he wouldn't consider returning to the Harry Potter universe in any way:

"[Potter] was great to do, but I don't want to go back to it. Honestly ... I feel like, in the same way that I feel about like all the jobs I've done, I loved doing them, and I hold the memories of them very, very precious and treasure them, but like I wouldn't want to go back tomorrow ... There's a lot more to do."

In that same conversation with the Associated Press, Daniel Radcliffe was asked if he has advice for the new actor playing the Boy Who Lived in HBO's series, which aims to devote one season each to all seven books from the original novel series. (The first season, accordingly, is titled "Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone.") Dominic McLaughlin will portray Harry; he's joined by Alastair Stout and Arabella Stanton taking over the roles of Harry's two best friends, Ron Weasley and Hermione Granger, from actors Rupert Grint and Emma Watson.

Radcliffe demurred yet again. "I'm really truly in the business of not giving Dominic advice. He's got it. He doesn't need any advice from me," Radcliffe said, pretty reasonably. "If I say something to you, then the next interview people have with him, they'll be like, 'Oh, and Dan said you should do this,' and I'm like, I don't want to haunt this poor child through interviews."

The Tony winner continued: "So I have no advice for him. He's going to be amazing, and you know, I hope that I will, I think I will, get to meet him someday — and I'm very excited about that."

So what has Radcliffe been up to since he hung up his Gryffindor cloak and put away his magic wand for good? Quite a lot, actually!

People can get pigeonholed into roles, and being a child star is exceptionally difficult. Somehow, Daniel Radcliffe managed to overcome both of those hurdles and become one of Hollywood and Broadway's most exciting and audacious performers. (I say "somehow" even though Radcliffe has been pretty open about how his parents helped shield him from some of the most difficult parts of fame at a young age — but still, it's impressive!)

Even during the original run of the Harry Potter films, Radcliffe worked extensively on stage in daring projects like "Equus" (a seriously dark drama that requires full nudity and allowed Radcliffe to act alongside Sir Richard Griffiths, who played his evil Uncle Vernon in the Potter films) and "How to Succeed in Business Without Really Trying." Despite his apparent aversion to television, Radcliffe has also turned in excellent performances on shows like "A Young Doctor's Notebook" (where, hilariously, he plays a younger version of "Mad Men" star Jon Hamm) and "Miracle Workers," and as far as films go, he's pushed himself in comedies like "Weird" and "The Lost City."

I'm most excited, though, about Radcliffe's starring role alongside Tracy Morgan on "The Fall and Rise of Reggie Dinkins." As Morgan's former football player Reggie tries to restore his reputation and potentially get voted into the NFL Hall of Fame, Radcliffe's Oscar-winning documentarian Arthur Tobin chronicles his life ... and the two are a perfect pair. I hope that Radcliffe has at least watched Morgan on "30 Rock" — but in any case, you can stream "Reggie Dinkins" on Peacock now, and "Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone" premieres on HBO on Christmas Day.

‘I Play Rocky’ writer says rival studios balked at ‘advertising another studio’s franchise’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sylvester Stallone first played Rocky Balboa 50 years ago this month. Now Anthony Ippolito is playing Stallone in I Play Rocky, a movie about the making of the original that began life without the studio that owns the franchise.

Directed by Peter Farrelly, the film follows Stallone’s hardscrabble push to write Rocky and his insistence on playing the title role himself. It premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival in September, where it was first runner-up for the People’s Choice Award.

It is also the first film from the Rocky universe to come together entirely under Amazon’s ownership, giving Amazon MGM Studios a fresh way to introduce one of its marquee franchises to a new generation. According to screenwriter Peter Gamble, that payoff is exactly why rival studios passed on it.

Rocky opened in November 1976, earned 10 Academy Award nominations, and won three, including Best Picture. It grossed about $117 million at the domestic box office. Along with The Godfather, Jaws, and Star Wars, it is often credited with kicking off Hollywood’s blockbuster era of entertaining, grandiose, myth-affirming movies that sparked billion-dollar franchises.

Five sequels followed between 1979 and 2006, then the Creed spinoff trilogy, plus merchandise from action figures to mobile games. For a roughly $1 million movie about a fighter going the distance, Rocky has stayed relevant across generations: the nine films have grossed about $948 million domestically and $1.9 billion worldwide, according to The Numbers.

That library is a big part of what Amazon bought when it agreed to pay $8.45 billion for MGM, home to both Rocky and James Bond. The deal closed in 2022, and MGM now sits inside Amazon MGM Studios. Its parent has since become No. 1 on the 2026 Fortune 500, ending Walmart’s 13-year run at the top.

The franchise has already paid off once for its new owner. Creed III opened to $58.7 million in March 2023, and it held the record for Amazon’s best opening since the MGM deal until Project Hail Mary topped it this year. But Creed III was already in production when the acquisition closed.

I Play Rocky was developed without the studio. Gamble wrote the script on spec; it landed on the Black List, then reached producers Christian Baha and Toby Emmerich and director Farrelly. In 2024, they took it to the Marché du Film at Cannes to court financiers, choosing the independent route over studio financing.

“Technically you can tell a story about Stallone because he’s a public figure, and technically you can show scenes from the film as long as you comment on the scenes,” Gamble told Fortune. “So in theory, you don’t need the rights to Rocky to make this movie, but just because something is technically true doesn’t mean that it’s the smartest thing to do.”

Dramatizing the making of Rocky might have been protected as fair use, but some of the original’s iconography, such as Bill Conti’s unmistakable theme, “Gonna Fly Now,” would have been difficult and expensive to include without MGM’s cooperation. Copyright law is not an exact science, and every reference to the original film would have put the production on a treacherous line.

Distribution posed the same problem, since a movie about the origins of one studio’s franchise was a hard sell to everyone else. “We tried to go to the different studios, but the studios had the problem of ‘If we make this story, it’s going to be advertising another studio’s franchise,'” Gamble explained. “They were kind of like, ‘We don’t know if that’s really the risk we want to take because a lot of the reward goes to someone else. This is really an Amazon MGM project.'”

Amazon MGM signed on to distribute the film in 2025, before cameras rolled, which cleared I Play Rocky to borrow from the franchise. The finished film uses Conti’s theme and recreates exact shots from the original.

If it works, the film delivers exactly what rival studios feared: a new generation of Rocky fans for Amazon. It has been nearly 20 years since the last Rocky movie, 2006’s Rocky Balboa, and 11 since Creed hit theaters.

The early numbers are encouraging. I Play Rocky opened on seven screens on Nov. 6 and grossed about $345,000, roughly $49,300 per screen, according to The Numbers. It goes wide on Nov. 20, as Rocky enters its sixth decade.

Is the Hope at AFM Real?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Box office breakouts and a possible federal tax credit are lifting spirits among indie dealmakers — but disruption still reigns.

Film execs heading to L.A. for the American Film Market may be feeling something they haven’t in a while: hope.

Disruption still reigns, but independent and specialty fare has had a run of breakouts at the North American box office, topped by this weekend’s launch of Godzilla Minus Zero. The sequel from GKIDS and Toho opened to an estimated $32 million, well above the distributor’s mid-$20 millions projection. Takashi Yamazaki’s follow-up to the Oscar-winning Godzilla Minus One (2023) reportedly cost in the mid-teens and is being touted as the biggest live-action foreign-language opening since 2004’s Hero, which bowed with $17.8 million. Related Stories Movies Brandon Sklenar on 'Iron Jane,' Body Dysmorphia and His Rapid Hollywood Rise Movies Claes Bang, Valerie Pachner Drama 'I Is Another' Sells Wide Ahead of AFM

It isn’t the only surprise. A24’s You Can See Everything, the Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim documentary about Elizabeth Holmes, is entering blockbuster territory, having grossed almost $80 million domestically. That’s a rare result for nonfiction in a theatrical market that has been unkind to documentaries, and the film is only beginning to roll out internationally.

Niche programming is delivering, too. Angel Studios’ faith-based Drummer Boy, from directors Ben and Joel David Smallbone, opened to around $9 million. Nitesh Tiwari’s Indian epic Ramayana: Rise of a Legend, handled in the U.S. by Sony, grossed an estimated $8.6 million in its first weekend, on the widest domestic launch ever for an Indian film.

Add this year’s indie horror run, from Obsession ($500 million worldwide and counting) to Backrooms ($400 million-plus) and the self-distributed Iron Lung ($50 million-plus), and the makings of a good-vibes AFM are in place.

For sellers, the biggest takeaway is that audiences are showing up, and that they are young. Sarah Lebutsch, head of sales at U.K. group Protagonist Pictures, points to You Can See Everything as proof that a smart campaign and the right timing can still produce a hit. “Look at what You Can See Everything is doing at the moment in the U.S. It’s incredible,” she says.

She is equally encouraged by who is buying tickets. “It’s great to see those sorts of box office results, and the fact that Gen Z are going, and Gen Alpha are going and seeing films together in cinemas,” Lebutsch says. “There is a big resurgence of that, which I think we should all be very, very encouraged by.”

Policy may help, too. California Gov. Gavin Newsom is pushing a major expansion of the state’s film and TV tax credit program, proposing to raise the annual credit from $750 million to $1 billion. The incentive has been crucial for keeping projects in the state. Newsom is also backing the bipartisan effort in Congress, championed by President Trump, to create a national film and TV tax incentive that would stack with each state’s own credits. For indie producers piecing together financing, having a federal layer on top of state incentives would be a significant and much-needed change.

The presale market, long AFM’s foundation, also appears to be holding up. “At Protagonist, we’ve always done very well out of the AFM,” Lebutsch says. “Presales have always worked really well for us there. The big buyers from across the world, they’ll all be there to pick up packages.” Protagonist will be shopping It’s All Right, with Paul Bettany as an aging rock star and Asha Banks as a rising singer who, unbeknownst to her, is his forgotten daughter from a one-night stand.

The online creator-to-director pipeline will be a major talking point at this year’s AFM. Seemingly all the hot young horror directors honed their craft, and built their respective fan bases, online before their theatrical breakouts.

“The film industry can certainly learn from creators that storytelling doesn’t need to be limited necessarily to strict forms,” says veteran horror producer Steven Schneider (Paranormal Activity, Insidious), whose Spooky Pictures recently scored a hit with Hokum, which has grossed $25 million worldwide on a $5 million budget. “Stories can begin online, narratives can be told, shared and expanded through shortform videos and parsed out over time in a way that builds awareness and excitement. Creators have shown incredible prowess in meeting their audiences where they’re at.”

But Schneider cautions that success online does not always translate to cinemas. “Traditional storytelling conventions and techniques are established for good reason,” he says. “We want to innovate and bridge the gap between an online audience and a theatrical one, but it’s important to respect the differences in the form.”

Traditional filmmaking, he argues, can “connect more deeply and profoundly with an audience through intricate storytelling,” and that craft “shouldn’t be dismissed in favor of virality or easy dopamine hits.”

Not every indie package at AFM will become the next Obsession or You Can See Everything. But audiences are returning, U.S. tax incentives are on the horizon and a new generation of filmmakers is packing theaters with younger fans. For a market that for years felt like it was in terminal decline, that’s reason enough to cheer.

Anxiety Is More Complex Than We Might Think

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 10, 2026 | Reviewed by Lybi Ma

Anxiety can be a necessary response to potential threats, but also a serious chronic condition that negatively affects our functioning and well-being. In either case, we feel wary and on edge, scared or irritable, with overactive thoughts. We are tense and easily startled into more powerful fear responses.

The key factor is whether our fear-system, our biological responses to threat, can ‘switch off’ once the threat has passed. If we hear a strange creak alone in bed at night, we might tense up, hold our breath and listen carefully; our heart beating faster. If we hear it again and recognise the hot water pipe noises, we can relax and quickly fall back to sleep.

However, if anxiety is a habitual state, we might still guess that it was the hot water pipes, but we can’t relax—we keep imagining an intruder breaking in—staying awake for hours in a state of fear.

What is anxiety? The biological response is at the core, what I call fear-alert, our instinctive reaction to danger sensed at a distance.[1] Our hearing, eyesight, and sense of smell become more acute to evaluate the threat, and our startle response is readied, and the nerve signal regulating our heart rate is withdrawn. The ‘brake’ that holds our fear responses in check is removed. We are now ready for extreme life-saving action.

This means our nervous system is highly volatile now; even minor or imagined threats can put us in a high-energy state of fear-arousal—fight, flight, or freeze. In this state, the heart's pumping capacity shifts up a gear, and a flood of hormones enriches the bloodstream with glucose and other nutrients, providing sustained energy.

Because fear-alert frequently switches into fear-arousal, our experience of ‘anxiety’ covers fear-arousal symptoms, such as a racing heart, dry mouth, sweaty palms, nausea or numbness, as well as the wariness, irritability and worrying thoughts of fear-alert.

None of this would be a problem if our fear responses deactivated once the threat had passed. However, this can only happen when we feel safe. If we are under threat and don’t feel safe for long periods, the biological processes that switch off our fear responses start to lose effectiveness, and we can get stuck in fear-mode, a chronic state of fear-alert that frequently tips us into fear-arousal or fear-collapse [2] [3]. This is another way of talking about complex trauma, which affects many areas of life, as we can see in the following example:

Adrian was a young man with a responsible managerial job who suffered from constant high levels of anxiety and was unable to make decisions about work and relationships, leading to constant self-criticism. He worked for an aggressive boss who treated him like a slave.

He found all social interactions stressful and exhausting; he felt the need to please everyone and keep them entertained. This burden led him to avoid intimate relationships, and the relationships he had didn’t last long.

He experienced all his pain and confusion as being "in his head," with his mind always overactive but his body “numb from the neck down.”

I worked with Adrian for nearly five years. He had previously tried CBT and found it unhelpful. I felt under huge pressure to alleviate his anxiety quickly, which triggered my own fear responses, but our work was painfully slow.

It emerged that he had suffered from anxiety since he was 3 years old. He had been highly disturbed by his mother’s acute distress at the death of her own mother. This loss of his caregiver was compounded by a sense that he had caused her collapse, and throughout his childhood he constantly tried to make her happy, becoming overburdened by responsibility.

Understanding the impact of his early history interested Adrian; however, it did not alleviate his anxiety, and realising that he didn’t have to take responsibility for the happiness of others also changed nothing. Being stuck for many years in a continuous state of fear activation had created a complex tangle of problems: nervous system volatility, chronic bodily tension, the numbing of sensation, deeply ingrained patterns of thought and behaviour towards others, habitual self-criticism and an inability to seek care.

We explored all these areas with little success at first. What started to shift things, however, was understanding and regulating my own fear-system. I stopped reacting with anxiety to Adrian’s pressing need for a cure and stopped overworking. I became a calmer presence, and he responded, eventually able to engage with ways of working that were not initially helpful. We worked on regulating breathing and using a focus-oriented approach that gradually helped him become reconnected with his bodily sensations. [4]

By the time Adrian finished work, he had become far less of a people-pleaser, had changed his boss and asserted himself more at work. He was in a relationship where he was gradually allowing himself to accept care as well as give it, and his generalised anxiety had greatly diminished.

Anxiety can be highly complex, and treating it at the level of a thought disorder that needs correction can lead to disappointment and frustration. Treatment may take time, and it may consider many aspects and use several different approaches. It also requires the therapist to understand and regulate their own anxiety in the work.

1. Also referred to as our ‘orienting response’.

2. For a more detailed explanation see my book Guilding. M. (2026). Fear in the Therapy Room: A survival guide for working with complex trauma. London; Hammersmith Health Books, pp. 45-50.

3. The metabolic shutdown triggered by the parasympathetic nervous system is the biological state underpinning depression.

4. Gendlin, E.T. (1996). Focusing-Oriented Psychotherapy. A manual of the experiential method. New York: The Guilford Press.

Xfinity offers free GTA 6 speed boost to download the massive 212GB game in 30 minutes

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xfinity handed its internet customers a free 15-day speed boost of up to 2 Gbps, so they could download GTA 6 faster.

The offer landed just as Rockstar flung open digital pre-loads on November 10, confirming the hotly anticipated game would need up to 212GB, or roughly a third of a base PS5’s usable storage.

With a download that size looming, Spectrum had already promised its customers a free gig upgrade, and it didn’t take long for Xfinity to follow.

Comcast’s November 9 announcement confirms eligible Xfinity Internet members can activate the boost through the Xfinity app or the Membership page at Xfinity.com/membership.

Once claimed, it lifts download speeds to up to 2 gigabits per second for 15 days, which Comcast says is enough to download GTA 6 in as little as 30 minutes.

The redemption window stays open until December 3, so anyone who claims it on the final day keeps the faster speeds until December 18. Meanwhile, members who don’t see the option on their Membership page can contact Xfinity Customer Service to request access.

Comcast pitched the boost as a way to beat the rush. Kohposh Kuda, Senior Vice President of Consumer Product Marketing, said millions of people would “hit download the moment Grand Theft Auto VI becomes available.” In fact, Xfinity expected the stretch from November 10 to 19 to be the busiest week ever on its network, with players barreling toward pre-loads well before launch.

The company also pointed members toward its year-round Boost a Device feature, which prioritizes the WiFi connection for up to three devices, such as a console, on a home network.

Saharan dust brings unexpected passengers to the Amazon rainforest

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Saharan dust carried a distinct bacterial community across the Atlantic to the Amazon rainforest, where airborne endotoxin levels roughly tripled.

The air above the Amazon rainforest looks calm from the ground. Under a microscope, it’s teeming with pollen, fungal spores, plant debris, and bacteria.

The fungal community is usually remarkably stable, while bacterial communities vary with height and time. But during five days in February 2020, something foreign arrived.

Saharan dust, blown across the Atlantic over ten days, carried with it an entirely different bacterial community. Its arrival also coincided with a threefold increase in airborne endotoxins.

The study was led by Jens Weber and Bettina Weber at the University of Graz in Austria.

They worked with researchers from the Max Planck Institute for Chemistry and a network of Brazilian partner institutions.

The scientists collected aerosol samples at the Amazon Tall Tower Observatory.

This is a 325-meter research tower surrounded by hundreds of kilometers of unbroken primary rainforest in Brazil’s Uatumã Sustainable Development Reserve.

Africa’s drylands are the planet’s single biggest dust source. They pump 11 to 15 teragrams of mineral particles into the atmosphere each year – roughly half the global total.

Those plumes regularly cross the Atlantic, depositing phosphorus and iron that fertilize the Amazon basin.

What wasn’t clear was which microbes the dust carried and how its arrival affected the air over the rainforest.

The team installed high-volume dust filter samplers at two heights on the ATTO tower: 42 meters, within the forest canopy, and 323 meters, well above it.

They collected filters daily from 10 February to 2 March 2020, then ran genetic analyses to characterize the bacterial and fungal communities in the samples.

The five-day dust event hit between 18 and 22 February. Backward trajectory modeling confirmed the air masses had traveled from North Africa.

The change was dramatic. Mean aerosol mass concentrations jumped nearly eightfold – from about 2.7 micrograms per cubic meter under normal conditions to over 21 micrograms during the dust event, peaking at 45.78.

The particles’ calcium content was consistent with Saharan mineral dust.

Under normal conditions, the bacterial community at 42 meters reflected local canopy sources – microbes associated with bark, leaves, and lichens.

At 323 meters, it picked up more long-range material. But during the dust intrusion, bacterial communities at both heights converged sharply toward the same profile: organisms associated with dryland soils, biological soil crusts, and marine environments.

Endospore-forming bacteria belonging to the phylum Bacillota – minor players under background conditions – became the dominant feature at both heights.

The researchers identified a distinct signature of terrestrial soil genera including Clostridium, Brevibacillus, and Tumebacillus.

Cyanobacteria characteristic of biocrusts – Schizothrix and Trichocoleus – showed up too.

Marine bacteria made the journey as well. Synechococcus, detected roughly 1,000 kilometers from the nearest coastline, illustrated the dust plume’s capacity to scoop up ocean microbes during its transatlantic passage.

The fungi, by contrast, barely noticed. Wood-decomposing Basidiomycota dominated at both heights before, during, and after the dust event.

At 42 meters, they made up 93.8% of fungal sequences.

The researchers suggest that abundant local spores may have masked any fungal material arriving from Africa.

“Soils in arid regions and deserts are largely colonized by microbial communities, known as biological soil crusts,” Bettina Weber said.

“Genetically, the bacteria match organisms from the Sahara, which proves that they are transported from African biocrusts across the Atlantic by wind all the way to Brazil’s rainforest.”

The most immediately concerning finding wasn’t a particular species. It was endotoxins – lipopolysaccharides found in the outer membranes of Gram-negative bacteria, released when those bacteria die.

They’re chemically stable, resistant to heat, and capable of triggering inflammation in humans.

Endotoxin concentrations at 323 meters roughly tripled during the dust event, rising from 0.45 to 1.27 endotoxin units per cubic meter, with a peak of 2.10.

The levels correlated tightly with coarse-mode aerosol mass.

The researchers traced the signal to two Gram-negative sources: dead microbial material from African dryland soils and marine cell-wall debris picked up over the ocean.

“I was surprised by the large amount of endotoxins in the air,” Jens Weber said.

“Previous studies in the Caribbean have shown that asthma attacks often increase when Saharan dust arrives. Whether a similar correlation exists in the Amazon remains to be investigated.”

Those endotoxins are relevant not just for the forest ecosystem but for roughly 3.9 million people in Brazil’s Amazonas state, plus riverside and coastal communities sitting in the plume’s path.

One critical gap is that the study can’t tell us whether the bacteria are still alive. Amplicon sequencing detects genetic material regardless of whether the organism is viable or dead.

Endotoxins trigger immune responses either way – dead bacteria are perfectly capable of causing inflammation.

But whether intact, living microorganisms survive ten days of UV radiation, desiccation, and temperature swings over the open Atlantic is a separate question.

The team plans follow-up work using fluorescence microscopy and flow cytometry to test viability directly.

There’s also the matter of pathogens. Obligate pathogens weren’t enriched during the dust event.

Some opportunistic genera – Staphylococcus and Clostridium – did increase at 323 meters.

However, identification at the genus level could not establish whether disease-causing species were present.

The dust appears to deliver soil bacteria and immunostimulatory debris rather than an army of infectious agents.

That distinction matters, though it won’t be fully settled until viability testing catches up.

The study is published in the journal Communications Earth & Environment.

DLSS 5 has been out for a while now, love it or hate it, but obviously not for AMD cards. Well, not officially, anyway. We have previously seen and tried mods that get the machine learning-aided neural rendering tech up and running using AMD RX 9000-series silicon, and now it looks like there's a significantly improved one.

AMDLSS5 version 2.0 is now out (via Wccftech), and it's claiming some impressive improvements compared to other DLSS 5 mods. Specifically, compared with "other mods", it's claiming +64% fps in Resident Evil Requiem, and +11% fps compared with the previous version of this one.

That's particularly great, if true, because our Dave's testing of DLSS 5 on an AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT using a different mod was, erm, not pretty. We're talking just 9 fps in Cyberpunk 2077, admittedly at 4K res on RT Ultra settings, but that was with FSR 4 on and set to Balanced.

It's not all the mod's fault, though, as DLSS 5 itself can be very intensive to run, even on an Nvidia GPU. Our Andy found this out when he tried running it in NBA 2K27. Frame rates there dropped up to 73% at 4K while using an RTX 5080, and there were significant drops at lower resolutions, too.

According to the mod's creator on Reddit, the following games are currently supported:

It's worth noting that some of the Reddit comments say the quality isn't very good with the mod enabled. That's to be expected, I suppose, for something unofficial, but it's just something to keep in mind if you're planning on installing it.

Installation itself involves simply downloading the files and running the installer. It then "installs, updates or removes the mod with one click."

GTA 6 is the ‘most pre-ordered game in PlayStation history’ despite the fact it’s locked at 30FPS, even on PS5 Pro

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Even a choppy frame rate can't stop the hype train.

It’s official, folks; Grand Theft Auto VI is now the most pre-ordered game of all time, and players who helped set that record can now start pre-loading it.

With over seven million pre-purchases and counting, GTA VI has been on track to become the most pre-ordered video game ever made for months. This morning, the official PlayStation account on X confirmed that GTA VI has finally reached that impressive milestone, formally dethroning Cyberpunk 2077, which held the title of “most pre-ordered game of all time” since its 2020 release.

Grand Theft Auto VI is the most pre-ordered game in PlayStation Store history!Mark the occasion with a video showcasing why GTAVI plays best on PS5Get ready for launch on November 19 by clearing PS5 storage space and pre-loading today: https://t.co/wTPa5E4DJY pic.twitter.com/WTQvHQs9cI

In a truly happy twist of fate, this groundbreaking moment synced up almost perfectly with Rockstar Games announcing through their website that GTA VI is now up for pre-loading. Anyone who’s already purchased a digital copy can now start downloading it to their console or PC, so it’ll be ready to play as soon as it releases on November 19, 2026.

As great as this news is, it also comes with a caveat. In the same post where they confirmed players can pre-load the game, Rockstar also explained that GTA 6 is locked at 30fps, even on the PlayStation 5 Pro, to ensure roughly the same quality across platforms.

Grand Theft Auto VI has been fully developed from the ground up to provide the best possible experience for the current generation of consoles, targeting 30 frames per second while pushing both platforms to their absolute limits to bring the vast and rich world of Leonida to life — while maintaining the density and visual fidelity of all its environments and inhabitants.

While plenty of gamers will be upset that they won’t be able to experience Leonida and all its unhinged beauty at 60fps, Rockstar confirmed that PS5 Pro owners will get a pretty crisp cut of the game thanks to PlayStation Spectral Super Resolution and higher ray-tracing support. It’s a small victory, but it’s a victory nonetheless.

In other happy news, Xbox CEO Asha Shar announced that GTA VI will be available on Xbox Cloud Gaming on both the Xbox Series X|S and Xbox One as part of a newly unveiled “ten-year strategic partnership” with Take-Two

Today marks the first step in our new 10-year strategic partnership with Take-Two. GTA VI, already the most pre-ordered game ever on Microsoft Store, will be playable on XBOX One through cloud gaming when it launches on November 19. https://t.co/WTtRs6plZx

As sad as some people are about the hard 30fps limit, it’s clear that the GTA hype storm isn’t going anywhere. So if you’re one of the millions who’ve already downloaded GTA VI, now’s the perfect time to get it onto your system.

MusiCares’ 2026 Wellness in Music Survey: 5 Statistics That Paint a Sobering Picture

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

The situation is even worse for emerging professionals (aged 18 to 24).

MusiCares shared key findings from its 2026 Wellness in Music Survey on Tuesday (Nov. 10), revealing that financial instability, limited access to healthcare and persistent mental health challenges remain closely interconnected across the music industry.

More than 3,300 music professionals responded to this year’s survey in English and Spanish, representing every U.S. state and Puerto Rico and more than 40 professions across the industry. The annual survey examines more than 100 indicators, including financial security, physical and mental health, access to health insurance and preventive care, substance use and confidence in sustaining a career in music.

MusiCares’ takeaway from the study: “Taken together, the 2026 findings point to a need for the industry to look beyond individual wellness interventions and more closely examine the underlying conditions affecting whether music professionals can build healthy, sustainable careers.”

This year’s survey also included dedicated outreach to emerging music professionals aged 18 to 24 with support from Live Free 999, a nonprofit founded by Carmela Wallace in honor of her son, the late rapper Juice WRLD, offering a closer look at the challenges facing the next generation entering the industry.

Since its founding in 1989, MusiCares has distributed more than $146 million in financial assistance to the music community. Findings from the Wellness in Music Survey help MusiCares identify where needs are most urgent.

In recent years, survey findings have helped shape expanded services, including access to women’s and family care through Maven Clinic, increased education around cannabis use, and scaled preventive care services. Following last year’s Wellness in Music data showing persistently high rates of suicidal ideation among music professionals, MusiCares also expanded its mental health resources through Tune Into Mental Health, developed with The Jed Foundation (JED) to provide mental health and suicide prevention resources tailored specifically to the music community.

Between 46% and 50% of music professionals who responded to the survey earn less than $50,000 annually, while only 30% say they can comfortably cover their expenses through work in music. Finances remain the leading single source of stress, anxiety and depression across demographics.

Approximately 84% to 85% of music professionals who responded to the survey report having health insurance compared with 93% of the general U.S. population. Coverage is lower among Black and Latino music professionals who responded at 79% and 80.6%, respectively.

Even for those who are insured, high deductibles, costly out-of-pocket expenses and limited coverage can make it difficult to access the care they need. Among those without insurance, more than 60% cite affordability as the reason they do not have coverage. Preventive care access also differs sharply by insurance status. Nearly 80% of insured respondents reported receiving an annual exam compared with just one-third of those without coverage, with similar disparities across dental, vision, hearing and cancer screenings.

Despite increased attention on mental health, the proportion of music professionals using available care has not substantially changed. Over the last five years, the percentage of music professionals who responded to the survey who report having accessed therapy has remained relatively steady at 57% to 59%. Among those interested in therapy who have not received it, 45% cite an inability to pay as the primary barrier. Suicidal ideation among respondents has also consistently measured two to three times higher than the U.S. general population.

Within the overall survey sample, 203 respondents were emerging music professionals (defined as those aged 18 to 24). With the support provided by Live Free 999, MusiCares was able to analyze this group more closely to compare their experiences with those of the broader respondent pool. Among them, 68.8% earned $25,000 or less in the previous year and 86.4% earned $50,000 or less. Seventy-nine percent said they could not comfortably cover their expenses through work in music, compared with 70% of respondents overall, while 72.7% reported low or very low confidence in their ability to cover future expenses through music work alone.

Health coverage among this younger group presents another important consideration. While 89.7% of emerging professionals who responded to the survey currently have health insurance, 57.4% receive that coverage through a parent. MusiCares’ summary: “As these professionals age out of family coverage, many may enter an industry where employer-sponsored insurance is far from guaranteed, underscoring the need to help early-career music professionals prepare for health care costs and coverage as their careers evolve.”

Want More Out of Therapy? Let Your Therapist Know

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 10, 2026 | Reviewed by Gary Drevitch

"I like my therapist, but sometimes I wish they would just ask me more open-ended questions."

An amazing colleague, who is also professional coach, recently said this to me. This colleague shared how her therapist is brilliant and helpful, but often shifts too quickly into problem-solving mode. I inquired if my colleague had ever given her therapist this feedback and she shook her head. I asked her what stopped her and she replied that she just never thought of doing so, but felt that her therapist would likely respond positively if she did. I encouraged her, in the same way that I encourage you, dear reader, to regularly be sharing feedback in your sessions.

Licensed therapists have years of training, including learning about evidence-based practices and getting supervised training in providing therapy. Your therapist shows up to your sessions with a lot of knowledge and skill that can help you recognize patterns, process significant life events, and so much more. But no matter how amazing your therapist is, one thing that they can never know, unless you tell them, is how helpful you find their therapeutic approach and the information that they’re sharing with you.

Therapists try to build a strong rapport with you, as they know that having a strong therapeutic alliance is important for you to feel safe and fully engage in your session. Research tells us that the stronger a therapist’s interpersonal skills are, the more likely their patients are to return for another session.1 Nevertheless, the therapist cannot know if you do not understand Information that they are trying to share with you, or if it just doesn’t feel aligned with your perspective, unless you tell them. You, your therapist, and your work together will benefit from this information.

It can feel awkward, giving feedback—especially if after reading this article, you’re the one who’s prompting such a discussion in your sessions. (Feel free to share this article as a jumping off point for discussion in your therapy session!)

If part of the reason that you don’t want to give feedback to your therapist is because you are afraid that they will stop liking you, that is also really important information that you need to share with your therapist. There could be many reasons for you feeling this way, and they are all really important to identify and discuss in therapy.

It’s normal for you to want other people to like you. This is especially true of your therapist, with whom you have shared a lot of sensitive information about yourself. Therapists have an ethical obligation to do no harm, to try to benefit you with their care, to build trust, practice fairly and justly, and respect the dignity and privacy of all people. This means that the therapist needs to be making decisions based on what they feel is healthiest for you and the therapeutic relationship. Therefore, even if your feedback is somehow given in such a way that your therapist could feel negatively about it, that is not a reason that you would not share it.

A therapists job is to take in the information that you’re sharing and to consider how the therapy session could be augmented to better meet your needs. They will not stop liking you if you provide constructive feedback, and if you perceived that were somehow to be the case, that would be a really important area for discussion and ultimately may signal that you and your therapist are no longer a good fit.

The feedback you provide can help the therapist engage in shared decision-making with you to see if you need a new treatment approach, if they need to refer you to a colleague so you can meet with someone who specializes in a different areas (e.g., couples counseling or EMDR sessions). This could mean engaging with different clinicians for different treatments at the same time or pausing your current treatment while receiving this care.

As a therapist, avoiding feedback doesn't mean you can assume a client is completely satisfied with the therapy: it more likely means you won't have a good understanding of the reasons why clients discontinue their sessions with you. Asking for feedback routinely normalizes the practice, helps you provide better care, and helps you learn and grow as a clinician so you can build your skills and continue benefiting your clients.

To find a therapist, visit the Psychology Today Therapy Directory.

1. Goldberg, S. B., Baldwin, S. A., Flynn, A. W. P., Babins-Wagner, R., Caperton, D. D., Williams, C. Y., Hamm, E. H., Lam, S. U., Cozart, J. K., Deole, G., Solomonov, N., Kopta, S. M., Anderson, T., Wampold, B. E., & Owen, J. (2026). Multimodal Assessments of Therapist Characteristics Are Largely Unrelated to Patient Outcomes: A Preregistered Analysis. Clinical Psychological Science, 0(0). doi.org/10.1177/21677026261424222

Bilt CEO Ankur Jain is betting AI and sports fandom can drive local spending—starting with free beers when the home team wins

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sports has never been bigger—team valuations are skyrocketing, fanbases are diehard, and the money in the industry is growing faster than it has before. Now Bilt, the online rewards platform, wants to make going to a sporting event about more than just what happens in the stadium.

The company is expanding what it calls a “neighborhood operating system,” allowing teams and local businesses to connect with fans through personalized rewards, loyalty programs, and AI according to a press release Tuesday.

The expansion is another step in Bilt’s effort to move beyond its origins as a rent-rewards platform and become an operating system for local commerce—under the proposed system, local restaurants and small businesses can use Bilt’s platform to pair with sports teams and drive local traffic through personalized rewards. The company has already partnered with multiple professional sports teams to make it happen—including the Phoenix Mercury, Phoenix Suns, Houston Comets, Houston Rockets, Houston Texans, Sacramento Kings, and Chicago Bears.

“We think of it as expanding game day into the neighborhood,” Bilt founder and CEO Ankur Jain told Fortune. Teams could offer fans restaurant reservations before games, transportation to stadiums and watch parties at nearby businesses—alongside exclusive discounts and other benefits, he said.

Bilt is also bringing sponsors into the equation. Jain described an arrangement with Constellation Brands, the company behind Corona, in which fans participating in a team’s loyalty program could receive a complimentary round of the beer at a local bar if their team wins. The sponsor would fund the offer directly through Bilt and the bar’s point-of-sale system without needing access to individual customers’ private information, Jain explained.

As pro sports have expanded past television screens in the modern day, they’ve become a source of community: fans congregating at sports bars, stadium tailgates, and camaraderie.

According to S&P Global Market Intelligence’s 2026 Consumer Insights survey in July, approximately 74% of Americans watch live sports. Of those fans, 53% of Americans watch the NFL or college football, 36% watch baseball or basketball, and 20% watch hockey.

And that large fanbase means larger commercial opportunities that can extend past ticket sales. Sports teams have created opportunities to monetize their audiences through merchandise, sponsorships, memberships, and experiences past game day.

The global sports market generated an estimated $463 billion in revenue in 2024 and is projected to reach $610 billion by 2028, according to a 2024 study from investment bank Houlihan Lokey. Sports-related travel alone generated $111.2 billion in direct spending in the U.S. in 2025 as 339 million people traveled to attend sporting events, according to the Sports Events and Tourism Association’s 2026 State of the Industry Report.

According to Deloitte’s 2026 Global Sports Industry Outlook, AI and changing ownership models converged with sports, media, and entertainment as major forces shaping the industry—which is “inclusive of leagues, teams, federations, mega-events, media partners, investors, and more,” according to the report. The firm highlighted the importance of sports venues as year-round commercial destinations.

“AI has outgrown its role as a trick play,” the report read. “For many sports organizations, it’s becoming a whole new game plan. Moving into 2026 and beyond, sports organizations looking to compete shouldn’t ignore AI.”

The rollout of the new platform also impacts small businesses, according to Bilt. Jain said AI is an integral part of making the system work, especially for small businesses that struggle to manage multiple software platforms.

Bilt’s “Neighborhood OS” is at the center of the new system that rewards users for visiting local businesses, an AI-powered platform helps local businesses attract customers, build loyalty, and grow without replacing their existing systems—“designed to unlock” a $6.6 trillion opportunity in “local commerce.” According to Bilt, approximately $6.6 trillion of annual U.S. retail and food-services spending occurs in person.

Bilt’s AI Agents have completed more than 2 million real-world actions across more than 29,000 businesses, with 91% completed without human intervention in the past 30 days, according to the company. In one example, Jain described a restaurant potentially using Bilt’s system to recognize returning customers, identify previous orders, provide special offers, and combine existing software into one app.

“To me, the way a lot of these technology companies are looking at AI, they’re all talking about digitizing everything and taking away jobs and living online,” Jain said. “But our belief is that the biggest beneficiaries of it will be small businesses who can now use AI to help them run their fragmented business.”

Joshua Hong is a News Fellow at Fortune covering data, AI, cybersecurity, energy, and retail.

The Impact of a Psychopathic Mother

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 10, 2026 | Reviewed by Michelle Quirk

When my children were young, I remember reading aloud children’s books that delighted both them and me. One book that especially comes to mind is Diane Goode’s Where’s Our Mama? 1 The author dedicated this book to her own mother, and her feelings for her mother are expressed through the two young children in the story. In the story, a gust of wind blew the mother’s hat off her head. When the mother went to fetch her hat, the two children became separated from her and began to cry. A policeman approached them as they searched for their mama and asked them to describe her. They quickly said she was the most beautiful and bravest person who always wears the prettiest hats, has a voice that everyone loves, and is the best cook in the world. Of course, they were all soon joyfully reunited.

As in the story, “mama” elicits an image of a loving, giving, selfless person. But some people never had such a mother. Many were born the children of psychopathic mothers. For those in such circumstances, what can they expect?

According to psychopathy experts, Paul Babiak and Robert D. Hare, in their book, Snakes in Suits, “Psychopaths may push the envelope of acceptable human behavior … . Some psychopaths live in society and do not technically break the law—although they may come close, with behavior that usually is very unpleasant for those around them. Some may lead seemingly normal lives, not hurting people in ways that attract attention, but causing problems nonetheless in hidden economic, psychological, and emotionally abusive ways. They do not make warm and loving parents, children, or family members.”2

Having had a mother and sister who were highly psychopathic, I had eye-opening experiences from when I was young. I noticed early on that both my mother and sister were different from other mothers and other children that I knew. But it took years before I was able to understand the term that described their personality disorder. Their behavior displayed the psychopathic traits of lovelessness and guiltlessness, which sociologists William and Joan McCord have characterized as fundamental to the disorder.3

In realizing this, I came to terms with the fact that my mother was the antithesis of the iconic mother. This realization has helped me to make sense of much of what I experienced and observed growing up. She consistently exhibited strange and unusual behavior. Previously, I could never understand why she hid and disregarded treasured family photographs. One day, I happened to find them hidden in a dark, clammy basement behind a piece of plywood on a cold, damp cement floor. They had not been placed there by accident. The age-old photographs were damaged by mildew and could not be replaced. When I asked her about it, she shrugged and was expressionless, showing her “I don’t care” attitude. She had no affection or regard for the warm memories the photos embodied. Instead, photographs of complete strangers were showcased in prominent places in the house for no apparent reason.

My mother had many strategies to get her way by creating conflicts and division between family members and relatives. I witnessed these conflicts all my life at different times and in many situations. One day, I happened to call my mother, only to find out that my niece had recently been married. My mother’s voice could not have sounded more delighted to tell me that I was the only person not invited. She perpetuated ill will and division. According to psychologist Neville Symington, “One of the most evident signs of psychopathy is the presence of confusion and bad feeling. One person is set against another and suspicion is rife, but the cause is never rooted out.”4

The brush-offs and the slights were countless, and the cold treatment never changed, even during a time of great personal distress. In such a time, even a stranger can sympathize with someone going through hardship. Instead, my mother and sister went to an astrologer to find out if my life would be ruined and, on a telephone call, they blurted out in high-pitched laughter exactly what the future had in store for me. They tried to get me to believe I would be ruined. I didn’t believe, and it never occurred, but their diabolical scheming was another sign of their disorder.

In dreaming of what a mother is, I came to grips with the fact that I never had a mother like "mama," and that my mother could never be trusted.

1. Goode, Diane. (1991). Where's Our Mama? New York, NY: Dutton Children's Books.

2. Babiak, Paul & Hare, Robert D. (2007). Snakes in Suits: When Psychopaths Go to Work. New York, NY: Collins Business. 19–20.

3. McCord, W. & McCord J. (1964). The Psychopath: An Essay on the Criminal Mind. Princeton, NJ: Van Nostrand. 13, 16.

4. Symington, Neville. (2001). The Response Aroused by the Psychopath. In Meloy, J. Reid (Ed.), The Mark of Cain: Psychoanalytic Insight and the Psychopath. (pp. 283–296). Hillsdale, NJ: The Analytic Press. 290.

5 Years Later, One of Anime’s Biggest Mysteries Has Officially Been Solved

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Okarun's backstory is Dandadan's saddest and best chapter

It's hard to explain briefly why Dandadan is one of the best manga and anime that have come out in the past five years. The series' eclectic nature and mix of genres means it escapes easy categorization. "The author is a genius" and "the anime adaptation is fantastic" could be reductive when recommending it to someone. Luckily, chapter 252 of the Dandadan manga, published on Nov. 9, 2026, does a much better job of explaining why this series is a masterpiece.

One of Dandadan's oddities is that, until now, author Yukinobu Tatsu has refrained from showing his readers anything about the background and past of one of his two protagonists: the sweet, spectacled, alien-obsessed Ken Takakura, nicknamed Okarun. This did not hurt the series nor Okarun's characterization, which was developed through his interactions with the rest of the cast, especially co-protagonist Momo Ayase. Now, we finally know that Tatsu had a long-term vision for this character — and it's a brutal one.

This article contains mentions of suicide and bullying.

Spoilers for Dandadan beyond the anime follow.In the current Dandadan arc, Okarun has lost his supernatural powers after fulfilling his pact with Turbo Granny. The foul-mouthed yokai has disappeared, and Okarun was left without means to defend himself when new enemies attacked him, Momo, Aira, and Jiji during a trip to a theme park. Okarun had to rely on the Ogre Club, a mystical weapon provided by Momo's grandma, Seiko, but it turns out that the tool has unpleasant side effects. Okarun lost himself to rage and attacked friends and foes indiscriminately, until Turbo Granny made her triumphant return. In chapter 252, "Ken Takakura," the spirit enters Okarun's mind to bring him back to reason, and in the process, she witnesses the boy's traumatic past.

Ken Takakura's parents split up after his mom found a new partner. She asked little Ken if he wanted to go with her, but he decided to stay with his dad. In a gut-wrenching sequence, Okarun remembers realizing his mom was actually relieved he didn't follow her, since he could have spoiled her fancy new life. Things kept getting worse for young Ken from that moment onwards. He was beaten up by other kids, bullied, and sexually humiliated. Meanwhile, his father was crushed by debt and experienced angry outbursts, leaving poor Ken a lonely, traumatized child.

One day, as he was running away from some bullies, Ken found an occult magazine that became his lifeline. He hoped and prayed every day that aliens would one day show up to "save his heart." They didn't. Okarun kept getting bullied as he grew up, until he couldn't take it anymore. In another chilling sequence, Ken is shown on top of a building, ready to jump off. However, thinking about distant, happy memories with his family made him lose courage (or rather, he found the courage to live), and he broke down crying.

After that suicide attempt, Ken kept getting abused at school. In the present, the influence of the Ogre Club mixes his memories with nefarious thoughts of murder. However, a voice (likely Turbo Granny) tells him that he has forgotten something: His prayers were answered. Someone did show up. The chapter ends with a panel of Momo sitting in front of Okarun's desk, shielding him from the bullies, a callback to the manga's first chapter.

At the beginning of Dandadan, in the much-discussed scene where Momo and Okarun are held by the Serpoian aliens, the boy said that, as he didn't have any friends, he looked up to the skies every day hoping that the aliens would show up. But they never did. Instead, Momo stood up for him. At that moment, this motivated Okarun to fully transform into Turbo Granny, breaking the Serpoians' bonds. It was a pretty traditional setup for a story that mixes romance and action, and it was hard to predict that the author would wait five more years before revealing the true depths of his protagonist's trauma.

Choosing to withhold his main character's backstory that long is a bold move for Tatsu, proving that he has confidence in his storytelling and character work. It also recontextualizes the current arc, which saw Momo lose her recent memories, including those of Okarun, which left Ken struggling to remind her of the bond they shared. Now, fans know that Momo isn't just Okarun's first crush or the girl who stood up for him at school. She's literally the person who saved his life.

Bullying, abuse, and suicide are difficult themes that are often present in manga and anime, but they're not always treated with the sensitivity required. Dandadan's long-term storytelling set up a big reveal about Okarun's traumatic past, but Tatsu has already shown that, despite his talent for action and comedy, he's not afraid to touch on other, more difficult themes. Vamola's backstory was, until now, one of the best and most traumatic parts of the manga (and one that I can't wait to see adapted in the anime). Okarun's backstory hits those same powerful emotional notes that make Dandadan a masterpiece and his author one of the best mangaka currently active.

Sundrop turns your endless baby photos into recaps, time-lapses and more — without AI

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New parents take a lot of pictures of their children, but those photos often end up languishing in photo galleries, never to be seen again after the initial snap and share.

A new app, Sundrop, aims to make those pictures more useful by turning them into weekly recaps, highlights, time-lapses of the child growing up, and other cute scenes, like putting the child’s photo on a magazine cover or a rookie card.

While Google Photos and Apple Photos already provide extensive tools to organize and manage photos, Sundrop is taking aim at a particular niche: Parents with a new baby (usually their first!) who take an overwhelming number of photos and want to do more with those pictures.

The app was developed over the past few months by the same team behind the read-it-later app, Matter: Ben Springwater, Sky Lan, and Rob Mackenzie (all dads themselves).

Notably, Sundrop claims it is designed to be private, and processes all photos locally on the device it is installed on. To make this possible, Sundrop taps into Apple’s Vision framework and on-device Core ML (machine learning) models instead of uploading photos to a cloud server or some other hosted AI service.

Both of the child’s parents can use Sundrop together by linking their accounts, which allows the app to sync their shared photos via Apple’s iCloud service. This feature, the company says, means the pictures aren’t sent to any external servers it runs.

In addition, the team notes that no generative AI is used in the creation of its scenes or highlights.

This differentiates it from many photo editing apps on the market, including Google’s, which are increasingly tapping generative AI to create new versions of your photos.

To find highlights and recaps, Sundrop scans pictures in the parents’ respective Camera Rolls on their iPhones, skipping things like screenshots or duplicates. It then creates highlights or fun scenes, which it calls “drops.”

One of these is a recap of the week every Sunday, announced as a Lock Screen notification. Others can put two siblings side-by-side at the same age, generate a time-lapse series of photos or a video of a kid growing up, or cut out a photo of the child and place it into a layered template.

Parents can also manually edit the photos Sundrop uses to make its creations, or use the app to automatically organize the best photos into galleries with other similar shots.

The app costs $39.99 per year, and offers a week-long free trial. The annual plan also includes Family Sharing, so only one parent needs to purchase it.

Why Man Utd Hired Erik ten Hag Over Mauricio Pochettino in 2022

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

It seems rather likely that Michael Carrick will be sacked as Manchester United manager during the current international break. GIVEMESPORT understands that Mauricio Pochettino has emerged among the three leading contenders to take over, along with fellow candidates Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann.

The Argentine is currently head boss of the United States men's national team, and won't come cheap, having recently extended his contract. However, there is a release clause of around $10m (approximately £7m), which could simplify the deal.

This isn't the first time Pochettino has been linked with the Man Utd job. In 2022, he was once again among the strongest contenders, but missed out with Erik ten Hag picked instead.

Ten Hag's time at Old Trafford was ultimately ill-fated. Although things started off well enough, with the Dutchman winning the League Cup and finishing third in the Premier League, things took a notable dip in 2023/24.

The club finished eighth that term on 60 points – which was, at the time, their lowest position in the top flight since 1989/90. A surprise FA Cup final victory over Manchester City kept him in the job heading into his third season, but he lasted until just October before being sacked in 2024, with the Red Devils languishing in 14th place with 11 points from nine Premier League games.

Fast forward to 2026, and Carrick is on the verge of being sacked, with Man Utd on just eight points after 10 league games, sitting in the relegation zone. Once again, Pochettino is linked with the English giants, but why didn't the club make their move back in 2022?

Mauricio Pochettino is a leading contender to take over as Man Utd manager, and he already has his own Dele Alli at Old Trafford.

As per reports at the time, Man Utd had three notable concerns about Pochettino, which made Ten Hag a more appealing candidate. Firstly, it was noted that the Red Devils feared Real Madrid would make a swoop for the then-Paris Saint-Germain boss at the last minute. They evidently felt safer with a clear run at Ten Hag, who was with Ajax.

Secondly, there was a simple matter of finances. It was more costly to try and prise the Argentine away from PSG than the Dutchman away from the Eredivisie side. Finally, the Old Trafford hierarchy were 'extremely impressed' in their meeting with Ten Hag, who set out clearly the steps to improve the club.

That third reason is quite interesting as Pochettino also met with the Red Devils during the manager search, but evidently didn't nail his job interview as well as Ten Hag. Despite that, according to The Athletic, the Argentine was said to be 'confident' of getting an offer, having already been courted for a number of years at that stage.

With that in mind, reports claim the coach was left 'shocked' when finding out that Man Utd had opted to hire his Dutch counterpart instead. It is also worth noting that the Man Utd squad preferred the appointment of Pochettino back in 2022.

Four years on, it looks as though the likes of Bruno Fernandes, Marcus Rashford, Luke Shaw, Harry Maguire and any other senior stars still on the books could finally get their wish.

These complicated business moves helped her network secure thousands of hard-to-get Nvidia GPUs.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang admitted to having an affair with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, who is also suspected of being a spy working for the Chinese Ministry of State Security (MSS). Now, an investigation by The Information has revealed a 'labyrinth' of companies centered around Shanghai Century Litong and connected to Singaporean firm Megaspeed, which has previously been accused of smuggling $2 billion worth of Nvidia chips. The report claims Alice Huang helped move 240,000 banned chips into the country, mostly H100 and H200 systems, and was instrumental in securing AI GPU imports to the tune of $7.5 billion.

Sources in the report link the two companies to Alice Huang via their personal knowledge and corporate registration records, while there have also been reports that some of the Nvidia AI chips Megaspeed bought went to Shanghai Century Litong’s data centers in China. But aside from these corporate ties, there were also numerous reports about Ms. Huang’s personal relationship with the Nvidia chief, and how it gave her the ability to procure chips that were otherwise hard to get elsewhere.

Ms. Huang allegedly boasted about her access to the Nvidia CEO publicly. People familiar with her said that she regularly uploaded photos with Jensen to her WeChat account and even shared other images with customers to establish her credibility. She even claimed that Jensen Huang would often celebrate her birthday with dinner and gifts, while an Nvidia China sales employee said that she was a familiar face at the company’s annual event for local Chinese partners.

When AI shipments hit delays or faced some trouble, Ms. Huang allegedly would step in to get them moving through the supply chain again. One other person who knows her said that she would often cite her close relationship with Nvidia and its suppliers as the reason why she could always get Nvidia AI chips. It’s estimated that $4.6 billion worth of AI chips have been imported through Megaspeed from 2023 to 2025, with another $2 billion routed through Speedmatrix. It was also reported that Shanghai Century Litong bought GPUs from other sources, with the assistance of Alice Huang if the orders faced an issue, with the total value of the GPUs acquired estimated to be around $7.5 billion. The report claims Ms. Huang helped move some 240,000 banned chips into China.

Alice Huang may have appeared in Jensen Huang’s circle much earlier than previously believed. One source told the publication that Kang Wei, Alice Huang’s husband, met Jensen Huang during the 2010s, when Nvidia was primarily a PC gaming GPU manufacturer. According to the report, Kang Wei was a private equity investor focusing on videogame companies back then. In contrast, Ms. Huang reportedly worked for two media companies in China, although some say that there were no records of her doing so. She eventually jumped into banking and finance, working at HSBC and then CITIC Securities, before finally entering the data center industry in 2023 with Megaspeed’s pivot and the founding of Shanghai Century Litong.

Shanghai Century Litong is a data center operator founded in June 2023 by a shareholder that was ultimately under the control of Huang Yu, the sister of Alice Huang, whose Chinese name is Huang Le. The company then provided compute capacity to AI tech giants in China, including Alibaba, Tencent, and other smaller labs. A few months after its founding, it received coveted Nvidia Preferred Cloud Partner status in October 2023, which is an important milestone as Nvidia requires anyone with this status to have demonstrated sufficient capability in handling and running Nvidia hardware as well as generating revenue at a certain level, although that threshold varies by region and partner type.

In exchange for this, partners get better access to Nvidia’s limited number of chips, which became crucial for Shanghai Century Litong as the China-specific A800 and H800 chips were banned for sale to China around the same period. Although the move immediately banned the sales of these GPUs from Nvidia, it did not affect the chip stocks that were already in the hands of distributors. This meant that Shanghai Century Litong had priority access to the limited supply of these AI GPUs as Nvidia’s preferred partner.

Megaspeed, on the other hand, was reportedly owned by a company called Huang Le Limited and led by Ms. Huang when it pivoted in 2023 and was subsequently sold to Singaporean entity Swiftdata soon after this. Megaspeed then set up a subsidiary called Speedmatrix, which has two offices in Johor, Malaysia, a couple of months after that. The Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS), the U.S. agency in charge of monitoring export control compliance, noted that the Nvidia hardware found in Speedmatrix data centers was still in its boxes when they inspected the sites, triggering a deeper investigation into the company. Still, it’s been alleged that Nvidia intervened in the BIS probe, with the company retaining its Nvidia partner status to this day.

Despite her public closeness with the Nvidia chief, she’s rather elusive when it comes to her personal information. Two other people said that she has always been vague about what she does at her companies, and that she’s careful when it comes to signing documents and leaving a paper trail that could easily be followed. Still, the Chinese embassy denies any links to Alice Huang, even as the FBI looks into the situation.

PlayStation wins "hall of shame" award from consumer rights group

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Choice, an Australian consumer rights organization, has named Sony Interactive Entertainment as one of its annual "hall of shame" award winners. Alongside a vocal group of gamers, Choice is calling out PlayStation for failing to give players ownership rights to digital games, a problem that will be much worse with the impending end of physical games on the platform.

PlayStation wins "the Shonky Award for asking us to pay to play, but not own," Choice says in its write-up. According to Sony Interactive Entertainment, the organization argues, "you don’t actually own the movies and games that you buy as a digital download instead of a disc. Instead, you’re buying a license to access the content via Sony’s various digital storefronts, such as the PlayStation Store."

In effect, PlayStation is offering nothing more than long-term rentals for digital games, since it can simply revoke those licenses at any time. That fear will be intimately familiar to players who've been following campaigns like Stop Killing Games, and it's not an arbitrary concern – after all, as Choice notes, Sony did just delist several hundred digital films, revoking digital purchases with no refunds as they did so.

"You can't protect your licensed media based on the current EULA, and there won’t be any alternatives once the discs go away," Choice concludes. "You'll own nothing."

That lack of ownership may be a feature, not a bug, of PlayStation's planned end of physical media. The company still makes significant money on physical releases of its biggest games, but analysts argue that it's more interested in "control" of the market. No discs means no used games, which means Sony can take a cut of every transaction and set prices – alongside third-party publishers – without worrying about competition from secondhand markets.

Choice's Shonky Awards have been ongoing since 2006, and have sporadically included gaming companies in the annual hall of shame. In 2023, it named and shamed the Xbox Mini Fridge for failing to actually cool anything while drawing more power than a full-sized fridge. In 2013, when EA's always-online SimCity reboot launched to global server issues, Choice called out a problem particular to Australian players: the support line charged $2.48 per minute, "making people pay to complain."

"There is no game preservation with DRM": Why GOG's leaders have spent 18 years fighting for classic games

AI-assisted firms increase flaw disclosures by 120% with no detected repair slowdown, study finds

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A preprint found a roughly 120% relative increase in disclosures against a Chinese-firm comparison group, with no detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied.

Software-flaw disclosures at firms given early access to Anthropic's AI model Claude Mythos Preview rose by roughly 120% relative to the change at Chinese firms without access, a November 4 preprint finds. Measured repair times showed no detectable slowdown.

Thomas Mosk's study finds that AI-assisted bug hunting was associated with more public disclosures without a detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied. Its findings cover publicly disclosed vulnerabilities and specific repair channels, leaving undisclosed workloads and users' installation of patches unmeasured.

Anthropic announced Project Glasswing on April 7, giving selected firms access to the model. The main disclosure analysis compares 13 participants with 267 Chinese firms from October 2025 through September 2026. The Chinese firms could use other AI tools. Selection into Glasswing was not random, and national disclosure practices differ, limiting causal interpretation.

Alongside the 120% relative increase in disclosures, the paper estimates roughly 150% more high-impact disclosures. That category means an above-median impact component in the vulnerability severity score.

Earlier Epoch AI analysis, published July 2, documented a spike in high- and critical-severity disclosures after Mythos Preview. It also warned that public counts omit undisclosed discoveries and can reflect increased interest in bug hunting.

Mosk examines fixing capacity separately, comparing Glasswing vulnerabilities with other non-Chinese vulnerabilities in shorter samples. One analysis follows 658 flaws reported to vendors by the Zero Day Initiative through June 2026. Another covers 14,855 vulnerabilities published through July and tracked by Linux distributions including Debian, Ubuntu, Red Hat and SUSE.

Neither repair channel showed a statistically detectable slowdown. For disclosed flaws, the distributions' median time to a fix stayed at one day. The vendor estimates rule out a slowdown larger than about 13%, although smaller changes remain possible. The downstream estimates still permit an approximately 11 percentage-point decline in the share fixed within 90 days.

For crypto infrastructure, the practical distinction is between finding a flaw, releasing a fix and installing it. CryptoSlate reported on Oct. 29 that Bitcoin Core disclosed three AI-discovered flaws after validation and patch releases. Installation across nodes remains a separate question.

The exploitation results also require care. In a comparison with large non-Chinese firms ending in May, before Glasswing's June expansion, Mosk found no statistically significant change in exploited-vulnerability counts across one-, two- and three-month windows.

The share of disclosed flaws known to be exploited fell in some comparisons. A larger pool of disclosures can lower that share without reducing exploited-vulnerability counts, however. Mosk treats the probability decline as suggestive; the count estimates remain too imprecise to exclude moderate changes in either direction.
