Why Everyone Is Suddenly Talking About Van Deer Skis

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Backed by Red Bull and fresh off Paula Moltzan’s first World Cup win, Van Deer is turning up in shops across North America. Here’s what we know about the skis, the price and the market. (Photo: Getty Images)

Overview: Van Deer is a ski brand created by racing legend Marcel Hirscher and majority-owned by Red Bull. Launched in 2021, it makes World Cup race, groomer, freeride and touring skis, priced from about $1,000 to $1,700. Paula Moltzan won her first World Cup race on Van Deer skis in Sölden. Distribution in North America is still limited, but the brand plans to open more storefronts this year.

If you watched the opening women’s World Cup GS in Sölden, Austria, you probably did a double take at Paula Moltzan’s skis. The U.S. racer won by 0.01 for her first career World Cup victory, and she did it on Van Deer-Red Bull Sports, a nascent brand she switched to this season. Van Deer is the brainchild of ski-racing GOAT Marcel Hirscher, and Moltzan’s win brought instant attention to a brand with a unique origin story.

The brand launched in 2021 and gained notoriety in 2022 with Norwegian Henrik Kristoffersen’s success on its skis, including a slalom crystal globe, an Olympic medal and a World Championship gold. Now Van Deer skis are suddenly turning up everywhere in places like Deer Valley and Vail—and even at big retailers like Evo. They’re not cheap: prices run from about $1,000 to $1,700, but it seems skiers are willing to pay. “Last year we had a phenomenal year with Van Deer skis,” said Billy Schoff, equipment buyer at Gorsuch, a high-end ski shop, which has sold them for three seasons. “We get people out on them once; they love them and then the word spreads like wildfire.”

So what is Van Deer, who are its skis for, and where does it fit in the North American market? Here’s what to know.

The details of Van Deer are deeply woven into what’s meaningful to Hirscher, who originally retired in 2019 (he came out of retirement in 2024, only to tear his ACL and be sidelined again). The brand’s name is a witty wordplay on his heritage—he’s half Dutch, half Austrian, and the German word “hirsch” means deer, hence: Van Deer. The H-Power 68 ski model nods to Hirscher’s Project 68, his initiative to get another win after his 67 career World Cup wins, even if he’s not the one racing anymore.

As a startup ski brand, it’s incredibly difficult to compete in a saturated ski market full of legacy brands, but along with Hirscher’s passion and precision, Van Deer has something other brands struggle with—resources. Hirscher was a Red Bull-sponsored athlete and maintains a strong relationship with the company. The partnership with Red Bull Sports, a multi-billion-dollar global brand, affords Van Deer-Red Bull Sports the ability to build their own factory stocked with state-of-the-art machines, hire top people to work for them, and support top athletes like Moltzan. (Red Bull has a 51 percent majority stake in Van Deer.)

“Marcel is obsessed with building the best-quality and best-selling skis,” said Schoff, who along with Dave Gorsuch was brought in early on Hirscher’s project. Moltzan sees the Red Bull effect from the athlete side: “Van Deer is still a new brand, so having Red Bull behind it makes a big difference. They provide a lot of support, which lets us focus on building the best skis we can.”

From the outside, Moltzan’s move looked like a risk. She had skied for Rossignol for 18 years and was coming off a season with four second-place World Cup finishes and an Olympic medal. “I know the change might look risky from the outside, but I was ready for it,” she said. “I was looking for new motivation and inspiration, and the chance to work with a team that has the ability and resources to build equipment specifically for me was a huge draw.”

She didn’t have a direct conversation with Hirscher before making the switch—they chatted briefly when she arrived at the factory. “I was going to be the only woman on the skis, and I knew there was a lot of work ahead of us, but that was also part of what made it exciting,” she said. “The first time I got on them, they felt completely different from anything I’d skied before, but I could immediately tell they were going to be good.”

Her husband and longtime serviceman, Ryan Mooney, was sold at the factory. “Ryan was really impressed when we toured the factory, the level of precision during the build process is certainly something that sets the brand apart from others,” Moltzan said. “He thought the powerful nature of the ski could be a great match for my style of skiing.”

True to its racing roots, every Van Deer ski has a World Cup construction and finish, but the brand now goes well beyond the race course with wider waist widths. Today there are five series:

Van Deer’s consumer skis are all unisex for now. On the race side, Moltzan says Van Deer didn’t start from an existing ski. “We’ve built completely new skis for the women’s side, so my feedback has been part of the process from the beginning,” she said, along with input from Mooney and Berni Arnitz, her second technician, who oversees women’s development. What makes them different? “Women’s skis are different from men’s skis, including the length, shape, and construction,” she said. “But I don’t think it needs to be more complicated than that.”

For now, finding the right ski comes down to length and model. “There are shorter sizes like 147 in H-Power 68 and 159 in H-Power 78,” says Max Wingard, Van Deer-Red Bull Sports race and promotions manager. “The SL model is the ski we’d sell as an easier to ski slalom ski. The GS Pro could be a masters ski, but comes in longer lengths.”

We haven’t had a chance to test the skis ourselves yet, but according to those who’ve tried them, they’re not just for racers. “I think the best customer for Van Deer is someone who really loves skiing and cares about their equipment,” Moltzan said. “You don’t have to be a racer. There are skis for everything from race courses to touring, but they’re built for people who want high performance and appreciate the details that go into a great pair of skis.”

Eric Lipton, National Alpine Team alumnus and director of Destination Ski Camps, skied the HP-78 and described it as “predictable and stable at speed.” “I’d describe the skis as having a race ski heart and soul in a consumer package,” he said.

The North American hardgoods market is a different animal from Europe’s piste-focused market, and Van Deer is making a clear play for more traction in the U.S. According to Schoff, the brand plans to build a North American warehouse, and it has recently hired George Couperthwait, formerly of Stöckli, to help build the brand and its dealer network here.

Schoff sees the target clearly. “I spent time in Austria with Leon (Hirscher’s brother, formerly of Atomic who now works on product for Van Deer) and his father, Ferdinand,” he said. “They were obsessed with building unbelievable race products and now the obsession includes commercial skis. They are doing it the old craftsman way, but with new machines that put on the best finish and even rip the sidewalls back. The quality of construction and finish is top quality and it’s clear they are going after high-end brands like Stöckli.”

Distribution is still limited, says Wingard, but the goal this year is to open more storefronts and grow the relationship with their existing retailers as well.

Wingard, who previously worked for Marker Völkl Dalbello and Stöckli, thinks Van Deer will catch on in both the East and the West. The line includes multiple narrow-waisted skis tailor-made for East Coast conditions. Out West, Van Deer will be pushing the Freeride 98 and 108, with former World Cup racer brand ambassador Travis Ganong giving feedback on the series and stores like Gorsuch in Vail, Aspen and Park City on board.

As a ski coach and former racer, I have a fondness for racey skis in a consumer package. You can feel the high-end construction and finish right out of the box, even in the way the bases glide on snow. A close comparison is Stöckli. For years, Stöckli skis have been a favorite among ski instructors, because skiers who focus on carving, like me, tend to gravitate toward skis with rebound energy, edge grip and stability. Van Deer shares Stöckli’s high cost and a degree of exclusivity, but the top-grade race materials and hand-tuning steps are meant to justify the price.

Van Deer’s advantage is that it has pulled together European craftsmanship, enticing long-time legacy-brand employees to come aboard, with state-of-the-art ski technology like the Wintersteiger Jupiter tuning machine and a brand-new factory, thanks to Red Bull’s resources.

All models except the Freeride 108 (which has some carbon) and the Touring series have a game-on construction: a double layer of Titanal and a woodcore of poplar, ash or beech. The Van Deer models available this season best suit strong intermediate to expert skiers who can drive a ski through a turn transition and want the ski to initiate a turn without too much effort, while still feeling edge grip underfoot and acceleration out of the turn.

You don’t have to ski like Paula Moltzan to appreciate a pair of Van Deers, but perhaps you aspire to turn like she does.

Current models are available online as well as at speciality retailers. Skiers interested in demoing or buying a pair can find a dealer on the company website. As for Moltzan, she’s racing around the globe, with Levi, Finland, next, followed by Killington, Vermont. As good as the skis already are, she says, they’re still a work in progress: “Skis are always evolving, so there will definitely be more changes to come.”

Take-Two Boss Says Ocarina Of Time Is “Terrific,” But Is It Competing With GTA 6?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Take-Two boss Strauss Zelnick has reacted to the release of Nintendo's recent Switch 2 remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time. He said the game is "terrific" and that Nintendo should be "very proud of the title."

But is it a competitor to GTA 6, which hails from Take-Two's Rockstar Games? Zelnick told GamesBeat that in the entertainment business, "You compete against everything and nothing."

Everything, he said, because consumers have so many choices about how to spend their time and money. And nothing because entertainment is not "must-have" like gas or groceries, so people will only buy if they feel compelled to.

"So it’s our job to make the best entertainment on Earth. When we do that, people will show up for it," he said.

This isn't the first time that the Ocarina of Time remake has been brought up in a conversation about the biggest games of 2026. Activision marketing chief Tyler Bahl recently said GTA 6 and Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 are the two biggest games of 2026, not Zelda. "No offense to Zelda," he said.

The Ocarina of Time remake is doing great so far in terms of review scores, currently standing as the highest-rated game of 2026. The game's sales numbers haven't been divulged yet, but its release is helping Switch 2 hardware sales.

As for GTA 6, the game launches on November 19 for PS5 and Xbox Series X|S. You can also stream it to your Xbox One as part of Microsoft's new 10-year deal with Take-Two.

Half of New Games on Steam Are Now Made With AI. How Does This Reflect the Gaming Industry?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Most of those games are unpopular, with 10 or fewer reviews from gamers.

Steam, the digital marketplace for PC games from game studio Valve, has hit a major milestone, sparking pushback from parts of the gaming community: Approximately 50% of new Steam games in October were made with the help of AI.

This underscores a growing trend in gaming, where more games are made with some form of AI assistance — specifically, generative AI, which developers have used to generate code, art assets, voice lines and more. Developers are adopting the technology in increasing numbers.

The numbers come from a Reddit user who goes by Ploz, and who used Steam’s publicly available data to see how many newly released games relied on AI in some capacity. This was possible because of Valve’s AI disclosure policy for newly released games, which requires developers to state whether they used AI in making their game. The disclosure is part of the storefront’s publicly accessible records, allowing anyone to view it.

A Valve representative did not respond to a request for comment.

The data is a snapshot of the tidal wave of AI-made games flooding storefronts, most prominently Steam. But crucially, Valve has chosen not to limit this flood, washing its hands of the problem and leaving gamers to fend for themselves in finding quality games. Developers had already been struggling to get their games noticed, and Valve’s limited curation seems completely unprepared for the firehose of AI-made games churned out on an increasing scale.

Valve implemented the AI disclosure policy in January 2024 to increase developer transparency. At the time, only 1.4% of newly released games used AI. This increased to 13.4% by the end of 2024, then to 24% by the end of 2025. It now sits at 49.9%.

There are some caveats. The AI disclosure is only required for games that use AI to create player-facing content, such as artwork, sound, character dialogue and localization. The 49.9% figure is specific to these types of games and doesn’t account for developers who use AI for behind-the-scenes tasks such as coding and bug fixes. A Google Cloud survey conducted in August 2025 showed that 87% of developers use AI for backend work, which doesn’t require disclosure on Steam.

So it’s likely that the number of games on Steam built with AI assistance is greater than 50%. Ploz notes that the only way users can avoid AI-generated games is to manually check for AI disclosures.

According to Ploz, the most common use of AI in game development is for artwork, which was mentioned in 63% of disclosures. Text and translations gobbled up 30%, while music (21%), code (16%) and voices (7%) rounded out the list. Many developers had more than one of those areas named in their disclosure, and about a quarter of developers said they were using AI for their Steam store pages and marketing.

About 3% of developers used AI directly as a game mechanic. One such example is a driving simulator called Teach My Little Sister How to Drive. Developers use AI in the game to interpret a player’s voice commands and generate the sister’s responses. To keep a playable demo of the game available to the public, the development team, Easy Fox, is paying $1,000 every day just to cover these AI operating costs — an expense so high that it was forced to take out a loan simply to keep the demo running.

For development, there are loads of options. The Unity 6 engine came out in October 2024 and includes AI code assistance, AI asset generation and third-party integration with Claude Code, Gemini and Grok. Anthropic’s Claude is a fairly popular option among smaller developers, since it supports the ability to vibe code whole games, with ChatGPT, Gemini and GitHub Copilot as other options.

Many of the games being released with the AI disclosure on Steam can be classified as slop — a derogatory term gamers have applied liberally to titles that heavily lean on generative AI for development and look derivative of other popular games. According to Ploz, most AI-enabled games are small: Around 47% of new games with fewer than 10 reviews have an AI disclosure. Among developers who released more than 10 games in a year, 68% of those games included the disclosure.

Games with AI disclosures received fewer positive reviews on average than those without. And among all newly released games with more than 1,000 reviews, only 17% include an AI disclosure. That means gamers are still gravitating toward games without an AI disclosure and pushing back against games that use the technology.

Developers seem aware of this. CNET reached out to two dozen well-known developers, including Epic Games, EA, Activision Blizzard, Ubisoft, PlayStation Studios and many others. All of them declined to comment.

Bigger studios may be using AI in their development, but it’s hard to tell the scale. There’s no such thing as an AI audit yet, and the industry seems to be relying on voluntary disclosure for now. Steam’s disclosure policy has outed smaller developers who are either worried about violating the policy and being taken off the digital storefront, or aren’t worried about the negative effects of disclosure.

A lot of dominoes had to fall for this to happen. The availability of AI tools and the increasing ease of use are definite contributors. Subreddits like r/AIGameDev have plenty of examples of people putting together games with just a few days or weeks of vibe coding, which is a lot shorter than the standard video game development cycle, which can stretch over years depending on the scale, production level and graphics quality. This practice has exploded over the last couple of years, resulting in a lot of games getting launched that are made mostly or entirely with AI.

Many of these games end up on Steam, which doesn’t have a policy limiting AI-developed games.

“Digital storefronts like Steam have already struggled to moderate low-effort and poor-quality games, as well as copycats stealing other games’ ideas,” said CNET Managing Editor David Lumb.

Valve openly admits its role in this, stating that the problem definitely exists but that the company doesn’t “want to be gatekeepers telling developers which tools they should or shouldn’t use.”

It takes a similar stance on AI cloning, with Valve’s Tom Giardino telling developers that “you must continue to grow your game by adding features, personality and creativity that stem from your own perspective as a developer.”

These policies essentially allow any AI-developed game or clone to reach the Valve storefront without pushback, which certainly contributes to the flood of newly released games with some form of player-facing AI involvement in their development.

Rival store Epic Games has a similar stance, allowing basically any game that wants to be there. The company declined to comment further about this, but CEO Tom Sweeney is an avid supporter of AI in video game development, stating that it was “perfectly logical” for developers to rely on AI to handle the mundane grunt work that takes up most of a developer’s time.

While Steam and Epic Games tend to take a more lax attitude toward AI slop, other storefronts like Itch.io are not. Itch.io has a whole policy against AI slop and the company has willingly, and at times gleefully, deleted terabytes of AI slop from its storefront. It also requires developers to disclose if they used AI but takes a much more consumer-friendly approach by letting users filter those out of search results if they don’t want to see them.

Some measures are being taken to address the proliferation of AI slop, but none will entirely stop the problem. Cloning has been a problem for many years. Mobile gamers of a certain vintage may remember the flock of Flappy Bird clones that appeared on app stores after the original game went down, and those weren’t even made with AI.

AI makes cloning much easier and faster, and some AI companies are addressing it. Anthropic recently changed Claude Opus 5.5 to prevent it from generating copies of existing games. Anthropic engineering leader Addy Osmani posted on X that using Claude to copy games infringes on intellectual property rights and violates Anthropic’s policy.

However, during CNET’s testing, we found we could still make games resembling popular titles, so cloning remains possible with a little creativity.

“This has come up over and over on the subreddit r/GameDev, and there’s no real solution if Steam and other digital storefronts don’t stem the flood of low-quality AI games drowning out the labors of love that entertain and innovate,” Lumb said.

Many developers openly oppose AI and refuse to use it in their own work. However, as the Steam stats show, that crowd’s output appears to be getting smaller and smaller every year amid the unending stream of AI games.

Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Security measures within XRP’s native ledger ensure no holder on the rich-list gets to take over the chain.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse used the company’s Swell 2026 event to address a long-running claim that Ripple Labs effectively controls the XRP Ledger simply because it holds a large amount of XRP. He said ownership of the token does not confer authority over network rules or validation.

The XRP Ledger currently has more than 150 validators. Of the 35 on the current recommended unique node list, only one is operated by Ripple, roughly 2.9 percent.

Network operators decide which validators they trust and can configure their own lists rather than relying solely on any single published set.

Unlike proof-of-stake systems, XRP holdings do not grant extra voting weight. Independent validators reach agreement on transactions. Protocol amendments require at least 80 percent validator support for two consecutive weeks before they activate.

BOOOM! $XRP Holders🚨🚨🚨 @bgarlinghouse CEO of Ripple debunked one of the biggest misconceptions about XRP ledger!For everyone still claiming Ripple controls the XRP Ledger because it owns billions of XRP, listen to what Brad said at Ripple Swell 2026.Owning XRP does not… https://t.co/zyDgmkPmIM pic.twitter.com/mhpDrqLnXG

Ripple can propose software changes and invest in the ecosystem, but it cannot unilaterally force upgrades.

Three amendments have been activated this year: Permissioned Domains on February 4, Permissioned DEX on February 18, and Batch transactions on October 9, 2026.

Notably, these tools are designed to support more controlled environments for institutional applications while still operating on the shared ledger.

Several highly established financial firms have moved forward with XRP-related products. Aviva Investors launched a tokenized share class of its Sterling Liquidity Fund. Franklin Templeton, DBS Bank, and Ripple have outlined plans involving tokenized fund shares and RLUSD.

Standard Chartered has also announced live institutional custody of RLUSD on the ledger, with additional collateral work planned alongside Ripple and LMAX Group.

These bullish developments do not depend on Ripple controlling the ledger. They rely on the existing consensus rules, low transaction costs, and the ability of independent parties to issue assets and settle on the network.

Even without control of validation, XRP retains specific functions on the ledger. Transaction fees, currently as low as 0.00001 XRP, are destroyed. Funded accounts require a base reserve.

The OG token can also serve as an intermediary asset for bridging different issued currencies when that path offers better execution.

Mr. Garlinghouse’s comments restate a technical distinction that has been part of XRPL design for years: large XRP holdings do not equal governance power.

Loris Karius joins Bayern Munich goalkeeper shortlist as club prepares for life after Manuel Neuer

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bayern Munich are reportedly considering three experienced goalkeepers as they prepare for a potential changing of the guard following Manuel Neuer’s anticipated retirement next summer. According to Sky Germany, Hoffenheim’s Oliver Baumann remains the preferred candidate, with Fulham’s Bernd Leno and Schalke 04’s Loris Karius also under consideration.

Bayern are increasingly expecting Neuer to retire at the conclusion of the 2026/27 season, although no final decision has been announced. The 40-year-old sustained a partial tendon tear in his right foot during Saturday’s 2-1 Bundesliga victory over Mainz and is expected to miss the remainder of the 2026 calendar year.

Neuer nevertheless intends to recover from his latest setback and return to action in 2027 rather than bring his career to a premature conclusion. With Jonas Urbig already earmarked as Neuer’s long-term successor, Bayern’s recruitment efforts are focused on securing an experienced goalkeeper capable of providing competition and support for the 23-year-old.

Baumann reportedly sits at the top of Bayern’s shortlist, with the 36-year-old Hoffenheim captain considered particularly well suited to the role. The experienced German international has established himself as one of the Bundesliga’s most consistent goalkeepers and would offer considerable leadership qualities alongside Urbig.

There are, however, potential complications surrounding a move to Munich. Baumann currently has 532 Bundesliga appearances and remains within reach of Karl-Heinz Körbel’s all-time record of 602. Moving to Bayern in a secondary role would significantly reduce his opportunities to pursue that milestone.

Baumann is also under contract at Hoffenheim until 2028, meaning Bayern would need to negotiate a transfer fee. No concrete talks between the clubs or the goalkeeper have reportedly taken place.

Schalke goalkeeper Loris Karius is the newest name to emerge in Bayern’s search for an experienced understudy. The former Liverpool goalkeeper has rebuilt his career at Schalke, playing an important role in the club’s return to the Bundesliga. His performances have reportedly attracted Bayern’s attention, although a move remains at an exploratory stage.

Karius extended his contract with Schalke until 2028 earlier this year, meaning the Royal Blues would also be entitled to a transfer fee. Fulham goalkeeper Bernd Leno represents another possibility. The 34-year-old former Bayer Leverkusen and Arsenal shot-stopper is reportedly open to returning to Germany and would be willing to accept a supporting role behind Urbig.

Leno’s current contract runs until June 2027, although Fulham retain an option to extend the agreement for another season. His potential availability on a free transfer would therefore depend on whether the Premier League club exercises that option.

The longer-term situation remains closely linked to the futures of Neuer and Sven Ulreich. According to Kicker, Bayern intend to hold discussions with both veteran goalkeepers before Christmas to determine whether either will continue beyond the current campaign.

Get German Football News has already reported that 17-year-old Leonard Prescott is increasingly likely to leave the club when his contract expires next summer, with the two parties holding differing views on his future. For now, Urbig will have an extended opportunity to demonstrate his readiness to succeed Neuer while Bayern continue evaluating their options for the next generation of goalkeepers at the Allianz Arena.

Kristallnacht' 88th anniversary: Merz says Germany faces historic test over antisemitism

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

It has been 88 years since "Kristallnacht", the "Night of Broken Glass" in Europe, a violent pogrom against the Jewish population across Nazi Germany and Austria, marking a critical escalation toward the Holocaust. Friedrich Merz, Federal Chancellor of Germany, said that Germany currently faces a historic test over antisemitism.

The Miami Dolphins (1-7) didn't just lose a game in their 19-9 defeat against the Detroit Lions in Week 9, but they also lost one of their best players. Defensive tackle Zach Sieler, one of the team's best players and team captains, ruptured his biceps and will undergo surgery with a projected 5-6 month recovery, thus ending his 2026 season, per ESPN.

Both of those numbers lead Miami in those two key pass-rush categories in the span of Sieler's Dolphins career.

BYD offices raided as JLR legal battle heats up over alleged Defender lookalike

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The raid took place at BYD’s offices in the Netherlands, where bailiffs acting for Jaguar Land Rover reportedly seized documents before a court case this week over its “copycat” Defender SUV, the Ti 7.

After BYD opened orders for the Ti 7 in Europe and the UK in late July, many (including us at Electrek) compared it to the iconic Land Rover Defender, with its boxy, rugged (yet still elegant) SUV design.

BYD even described it as having “an unapologetically boxy profile” that delivers “the imposing, chunky design that finds favour among SUV buyers, yet delivers a contemporary look.”

Apparently, JLR has also taken notice. According to a report from The Times, court bailiffs acting on behalf of the British luxury automaker raided BYD’s offices in the Netherlands and seized digital records.

The raid comes ahead of a preliminary court ruling on Wednesday, which will determine whether BYD’s Ti7 SUV infringes on the intellectual property of the Land Rover Defender.

JLR believes BYD stripped down the Defender to reverse engineer some of its technology. Before the raid, BYD claims it fully cooperated with JLR’s requests.

According to local reports, BYD’s lawyers called JLR’s raid a “fishing expedition” and claimed, “too many files were seized.”

JLR declined to comment, while BYD said it had no comment at this time as it’s in an ongoing legal case.

The Ti 7 is on sale starting at £47,995 ($64,000) in the UK, on the road. That’s over £20,000 ($26,500) less than the plug-in Defender 110, which starts at £71,840 ($95,500) in the UK.

It has a seven-seat arrangement, which BYD said was specifically designed for the European and UK markets.

As the first vehicle in the UK based on its DM-p (Dual Mode performance) powertrain, BYD said the Ti 7 “vastly exceeds the closest competition,” seemingly another jab at the Defender.

The setup uses three electric motors and a 1.5-liter turbocharged engine, delivering a combined 402 hp (408 PS), enabling it to accelerate from 0 to 62 mph in 4.8 seconds.

The engine primarily powers a 35.6 kWh BYD Blade Battery, which provides a pure-electric WLTP driving range of up to 74 miles. In the city, it has an electric range of up to 102 miles.

Available exclusively in the UK in the flagship Excellence AWD trim, BYD claims that the Ti 7 offers “a level of equipment that exceeds anything that its rivals can offer at this price point.”

With the preliminary ruling scheduled for Wednesday, we should learn more about the BYD and JLR legal battle soon. Check back for updates as this is an ongoing story.

McTominay reveals heart treatment at World Cup

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

McTominay reveals he underwent heart procedure days before World Cup game

Scott McTominay says he received treatment to correct an irregular heartbeat two days before Scotland's World Cup win over Haiti in June.

The Napoli midfielder recently underwent an ablation procedure - a minimally invasive operation to correct irregular heart rhythms - in Manchester.

At the World Cup, the 29-year-old was was put to sleep to correct his heartbeat and given the all-clear to play in the opening 1-0 victory over Haiti in Massachusetts.

"I had to go and get the shock on my heart," revealed the former Manchester United player, who also also played 90 minutes in the losses to Morocco and Brazil.

"It was two days before the Haiti game. You've prepared so well. I was in such good condition and then something like that happens. You're thinking, 'how is this happening to me?'

"You have to get on with it, that's the cards that you're dealt in football. Sometimes, things don't go your way.

"I was 100% [fit], but obviously, whenever you're sat in a hospital bed two days before the first game of the World Cup about to get put to sleep, it's not ideal, is it?"

McTominay praised his club and the medical staff who treated him in the autumn.

He missed Scotland's opening four Nations League fixtures - a win, a draw and two defeats - but has been recalled to the squad after returning to domestic and Champions League action with Napoli.

Sebastien Pocognoli's side host North Macedonia on Friday and visit Switzerland on Monday.

"Yeah, I'm good," said McTominay. "I'm feeling much, much better than what I was. I feel like it was something that needed to be done. In your career, you have moments like that where things can take you a bit by surprise.

"Obviously, it looks a lot worse to people in the media and family and friends who reach out to you after they see news related to a heart issue, but it's a relatively straightforward procedure, which went really, really well in Manchester.

"So just really happy to be back and playing football again. That's the most important thing in my life and it always will be in terms of wanting to come back and prove myself again and making sure that I can go to another level."

Nations League: Scotland v North Macedonia

McTominay added: "For me, it's always about coming back stronger.

"No matter how well you play in one game, it's about the next game and pushing yourself and recovering well and eating the right things, sleeping correctly throughout your career."

Lotus’s New V-8 Ferrari Fighter Is Coming in 2028; Here’s the Engine That Will Power It

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Horse Powertrains has pulled the wraps off its first eight-cylinder powerplant, destined for the Lotus Type 135.

Back in May, Lotus revealed its Focus 2030 strategy for the future, in which the storied British racing and sports car brand aims to return to its roots by “protecting what makes Lotus, Lotus.” What makes Lotus Lotus is, of course, at least partially combustion-powered performance cars that follow Colin Chapman’s maxim to “simplify and add lightness.” As a thrilling nod in that direction, Lotus confirmed plans to launch its first supercar, the Type 135—which may become the new Esprit.

Now, we now know what will power it. On Tuesday, Horse Powertrain—a Geely subsidiary alongside Lotus—unveiled its new Horse W40 powerplant, which the company has confirmed the will power the Lotus Type 135. Horse also manufactures the new V-6 engine that will replace the supercharged Toyota V-6 in the Emira sports car.

The Horse W40 is a twin-turbocharged 4.0-liter V8 with a hot-vee configuration, designed for use in high-performance hybrid vehicle platforms. It weighs just 441 pounds, but it can produce an impressive peak output of over 670 horsepower and 590 pound-feet of torque all on its own. When paired with a hybrid component, Lotus said the Type 135 will be able to deliver more than 986 hp.

The Horse W40 uses a proprietary 8LDCT transmission. It’s an eight-speed transmission with a “sportscar-grade wet dual clutch.” Horse Powertrain claims a shift response time of less than 20 milliseconds, and the transmission can handle up to 774 pound-feet of torque.

Lotus has not yet provided full details about the Type 135—those will be announced before the end of 2026—but the company has confirmed it plans to launch the new machine in 2028. The Type 135 will be all-new, distinct from the Emira; Lotus expects to build the supercar in Europe, unlike the China-made Eletre and Emeya EVs, which may reduce the potential exposure to American tariffs. Lotus has revealed a teaser image of the new car, showing a dual exhaust and a large diffuser.

The Lotus Type 135 may not be the only application for this engine, either. Horse Powertrain says the engine’s low-end torque could be used for trucks and off-road vehicles, if you’ve been keeping a candle lit for Geely-owned Volvo to build a raucous but Scandinavian minimalist Mercedes G-Wagen competitor. (Hey, everyone else seems to be doing it...)

Ford Teases Reborn Cobra R That Picks Up Where The Shelby GT350 Left Off

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Automotive  |  Read Source →

Folks, the Cobra is back. In a 36-second teaser posted by Ford Racing on X, the everything app, the Blue Oval said an "all-motor heart born from racing" is on the way for the Mustang, and if you read between the lines, "all-motor" means naturally aspirated. The engine audio clip playing in the background certainly lends credibility to that idea. With a 2027 launch date, there aren't any technical details to speak of, but the name "Cobra" gives me a lot of confidence this bad boy is going to sit above the GT and possibly even the Dark Horse.

You'll want to hear this. For the drivers, with an all-motor heart born from racing.  Ford Mustang Cobra, a Legacy Reborn.  The story continues in 2027. pic.twitter.com/fogI982jfl

— Ford Racing (@FordRacing) November 10, 2026

Of course, nothing is official, but leaked dealer information lends some credence to how this new Cobra will be positioned within the Mustang lineup. You can think of it as the "spiritual successor to the Mustang Shelby GT350," according to Ford Authority, and it'll launch next year as a 2028. Get it while you can, though, because it's expected to have a relatively short production run through the 2029 model year. Under the hood will be a naturally aspirated V8, obviously, and it's either going to be 5.2 or 5.4 liters, depending on how much freedom Ford feels like dispersing. Don't worry, it's also expected to have a Tremec six-speed manual transmission.

There's no word on horsepower numbers just yet, but we can look at the data as a guiding light. Currently, the Dark Horse's 5.0-liter V8 pumps out 500 horsepower and 418 pound-feet of torque. The Dark Horse SC takes those numbers all the way up to 795 and 660, respectively. Looking back, the 5.2-liter Voodoo V8 in the GT350 produced 526 hp and 429 lb-ft. If I were a betting man, I'd put my money on the horsepower number being somewhere around the 550-575 mark.

It has been quite a while since the Cobra name appeared on a Mustang from Ford. The last time it popped up was in the early 2000s supercharged SN95 Mustang SVT Cobra, which was a beast for the time. Since then, there have been some Shelby-specific Cobras, but nothing from Ford itself.

You may be asking yourself, "Why did Ford revive the name Cobra rather than just call the Shelby GT350's successor a Shelby GT350?" Well, it's because of money. As we explained earlier this year, Ford has to pay to use the name Shelby on its cars, and it doesn't want to anymore. Shelby America owns the name. It's the same reason the Dark Horse SC has its name rather than Shelby GT500. It hasn't been cheap, either. Ford was paying about $800 in royalties per Shelby Mustang it sold, racking up over $30 million in licensing fees in the process.

At the end of the day, it doesn't really matter what this thing is going to be called. Of course, "Cobra" is sick as hell, but it could be called the Ford Mustang Beige Wallpaper for all I care. It's going to be sick as hell.

Arctic Town Kills Dozens of Grizzlies Feeding on Dump

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For years, the grizzlies near Inuvik in Canada’s western Arctic relied on a stable source of food — the town dump. Generations of bears grew up feeding there. In 2025, wildlife officials found that over 40 grizzlies regularly visited the dump.

In 2025, before the fence was built, trail cameras captured multiple grizzlies hanging around the dump, looking for food. Photo: Government of the Northwest Territories

It was too much. This year, the town built an electric fence around the site, hoping the bears would move on. They didn’t. Some started coming into town looking for food. Others just hung around the fence, searching for a way in.

“They would just do these laps around the dump, and they would just be emaciated, sickly,” said Toby Halle, a Renewable Resources officer based in Inuvik, told the CBC. “They don’t know what to do with themselves. And quite frankly, we don’t know what to do with them.”

Inuvik, population about 3,500, was the first community in that part of the Arctic to try to wean the bears off their dependence on dump food with such a fence, which cost about $700,000. The problem was, the bears had not learned to depend on any other source of food.

“We’ve probably had three to four generations of bears that have been using that landfill,” said local mayor Peter Clarkson. In the end, the town took a drastic solution — but one that was probably unavoidable in the circumstances: They shot 28 bears.

Tragic though this is, officials hope that within the next couple of years, the remaining bears in the area will move on and learn to eat more natural fare.

Many other Arctic towns still have open dumps where bears come to feed. Photo: Government of the Northwest Territories

Jerry Kobalenko is the editor of ExplorersWeb. One of Canada’s premier arctic travelers, he is the author of The Horizontal Everest and Arctic Eden, and has just finished a book about adventures in Labrador. In 2018, he was awarded the Polar Medal by the Governor General of Canada and in 2022, he received the Queen’s Platinum Jubilee Medal for services to exploration.

How Spanish Media Feel About Julian Alvarez After Failed Arsenal Move

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

It was perhaps the biggest saga of the summer: Julian Alvarez had his heart set on leaving Atletico Madrid, but he was unable to secure a move away.

The Argentine's preference was Barcelona, but his club were unwilling to let him move to their La Liga rivals. The forward's desire to leave was so public – as was Barca's interest – Atletico even released an explosive statement, insisting he was not for sale:

“The player is being victimised, but the only victim of the Julian case is us. They have harmed us economically and socially.

“There is zero per cent chance of selling him to Barca. This situation is not about money, it’s about dignity. There is zero per cent chance of selling it to Barca."

With Arsenal in the market for a forward, the Gunners were ready to land Alvarez if he gave up on his Barcelona dream. However, the Argentine didn't seem open to leaving Spain and with Atletico true to their word, he was not sold.

The summer transfer window closed two months ago. Since then, Alvarez has slowly been reintegrated back into the team. Here's a look at how he's getting on.

When the 2026/27 season started, the transfer market was still open. This made for an extremely messy opening few games. Although Alvarez missed Atletico's opening La Liga game, he was involved in their next game at home against Villarreal.

He played 24 minutes off the bench in a 2-2 draw at the Metropolitano Stadium, and was booed by his own fans when entering the pitch. There were even reports that club CEO Miguel Angel Gil Marin insisted that the Argentine feature, as some kind of punishment, knowing that he would be jeered by the Atletico faithful.

It was suggested that this 'humiliation therapy' would force Alvarez to accept an offer from Arsenal, thus allowing Atleti to cash in on the clearly unhappy player, but not to Barcelona. However, the transfer deadline passed and no move occurred.

Since then, Alvarez has played 11 more games for his side, with four coming in the Champions League. He has just one goal and three assists, with seven starts in a total of 12 appearances.

He played the full 90 minutes in the recent home loss to Barcelona, picking up an assist. Following the defeat, the Spanish media made their feelings on the player very clear.

The La Liga side are paying some of their players very handsomely

Although he picked up an assist for Giuliano Simeone's goal, Alvarez also missed two clear chances. These included a one-on-one with Barca goalkeeper Joan Garcia, as well as a moment where he skied a big opportunity when through on goal again.

The Spanish press, via Goal, were not impressed. Journalist Manu Carreno was particularly sharp on Cadena SER radio:

"He is still unable to prove that he is worth 100 or 150 million euros. Atletico needs a striker who decides matches."

Former player Kiki Narvaez highlighted missed chances, saying: "They are two very clear chances, to the point that there was not even any reason to shout."

El Pais journalist Ladislao Monino said: "Given his technical abilities, it is not normal for Julian Alvarez to waste two chances as clear as that, as he did." While analyst Nacho Valencia noted on Radio Marca:

"You leave feeling like you threw your money in the bin."

With just months to go until the January transfer market opens, it will be interesting to see if Alvarez pushes to leave Atletico in the winter. If so, it remains to be seen if he only has eyes for Barcelona once more, or if a move to Arsenal could be on the cards.

Millie Bobby Brown To Star In, Produce Netflix Comedy ‘Non-Refundable’; Jack Innanen Also Set

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: After wrapping up Stranger Things and capping off an Enola Holmes trilogy, and with a David Harbour co-starring spy comedy upcoming, Millie Bobby Brown has found her next project with Netflix: a comedy titled Non-Refundable that she’ll produce and headline opposite Jack Innanen, the breakout star of FX’s Adults.

With Emmy-nominated SNL alum Paul Briganti aboard to direct, Non-Refundable is billed as a high-concept commercial comedy centered on Nate (Innanen) and Dani (Brown), an ex-couple who are still on the hook for their wedding venue despite breaking up. This leads them to travel to Mexico out of spite, where they try showing each other up with new dates. Related Stories News 'The Perfect Lie' Actor Matthijs Van De Sande Bakhuyzen To Lead AFM-Bound Arctic Thriller 'The Art Of Survival' News 'Swapped' Composer Siddhartha Khosla Used Music From Every Continent For Netflix's Animated Adventure - Sound & Screen Film

Pic is based on a spec by David Bernstein. Bobby Brown and Jake Bongiovi will produce for PCMA (Damsel, Enola Holmes), alongside Andrew Swett and Aaron Ryder for Embrace Entertainment (Netflix’s Office Romance) and Will Speck and Josh Gordon for Speck + Gordon Inc. Executive producers are Bobby Brown for PCMA, Eric Fineman and David Kern. Watch on Deadline

Sources said Non-Refundable reflects Netflix’s interest in revitalizing a version of the rom-com you see less of these days — a film like Forgetting Sarah Marshall that had big laughs and emotional stakes in equal measure. Execs were on the lookout for fresh takes and knew they’d found the one in new writer Bernstein’s spec when the team started quoting its one-liners and referencing its set pieces around the office.

At Netflix, Brown has become synonymous with megahit genre fare like Stranger Things, Damsel and the Enola Holmes films. But execs thought of her after seeing her performance in Just Picture It, an upcoming Netflix rom-com where she stars opposite The Fabelmans’ Gabriel LaBelle, which speaks to underappreciated comedic chops.

Innanen wowed Netflix execs with his turn as Paul Baker in Adults, an FX hang-out sitcom compared to a Gen Z version of Friends or New Girl.

Meanwhile, Briganti was the choice to direct given his work at SNL spanning six seasons, where he demonstrated an eye for surprising, off-the-wall humor, as well as a knack for getting the best out of talent. During his time there as segment director across Digital Shorts and more, select highlights included working on breakout Please Don’t Destroy videos, including “Three Sad Virgins” (feat. Taylor Swift), and directing Brad Pitt to an Emmy nomination via a Covid-era cold open segment where he played Dr. Anthony Fauci.

Wrapping up her five seasons with Stranger Things on New Year’s Eve 2025, Bobby Brown is fresh off Enola Holmes 3, which nabbed over 20M views in its first five days alone. Upcoming, she’s set to reunite with her Stranger Things dad David Harbour on a father-daughter espionage thriller for Netflix from Adolescence‘s Jack Thorne & A24. She is repped by WME and Hansen, Jacobson.

Seen over the summer in the second season of the critically acclaimed Adults — which seemed to have broadened awareness around the series — Innanen has also previously been seen starring opposite Dan Levy in Netflix’s crime comedy Big Mistakes from Levy and Rachel Sennott. The Canadian actor is repped by UTA, Range Media Partners and Sloane, Offer, Weber & Dern.

Briganti reteamed with Please Don’t Destroy post-SNL on the Peacock comedy Please Don’t Destroy: The Legend of Foggy Mountain. He previously intersected with Innanen as the director of multiple episodes of Adults and began his career making videos for CollegeHumor, where he went on to become site director. He garnered early attention for the Hugo Award-nominated satirical video “Fuck Me, Ray Bradbury,” starring Crazy Ex-Girlfriend’s Rachel Bloom, before going on to work on series including The Late Late Show with James Corden, Adam Ruins Everything, Crazy Ex-Girlfriend, Chad and At Home with Amy Sedaris, to name a few. He is repped by WME, Mosaic and Ginsburg Daniels.

Speck and Gordon, the filmmakers behind Blades of Glory and Office Christmas Party, are repped by 3 Arts and CAA.

BMG’s Concord Originals Preps Supernatural Gothic Horror Pic ‘Wolf at the Door’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The five-year-old division is taking a 360-degree approach to an expanded portfolio following Concord's merger with music and theater powerhouse BMG and acquisition of RKO's classic film library

The full moon is out at Concord Originals, the film and TV arm of music and theater powerhouse BMG, where it is developing and producing supernatural gothic horror feature “Wolf at the Door.”

Based on the eponymous play from Concord Theatricals, the genre pic will be helmed by Argentina’s Agustina San Martín, who recently directed the psychological horror feature “Psyche,” starring Noah Jupe (“A Quiet Place”) and Isabela Merced (“The Last of Us”), currently in post. Related Stories Olivia Rodrigo, Katseye and Sienna Spiro to Be Honored at Variety’s 10th Annual Hitmakers Event Raye Named Variety’s 2026 Hitmaker of the Year

The adaptation is written by playwright, poet and screenwriter Marisela Treviño Orta, based on her play “Wolf at the Door,” part of a trilogy of theatrical productions inspired by Latinx mythology and the Brothers Grimm fairy tales.

Set in 19th century New Mexico, it centers on a young woman, close to giving birth to her first child, who foretells the return of a malevolent half-man half-wolf creature, only to discover that another predator has already entrenched itself in her hacienda. Casting is underway.

“Wolf at the Door” is produced by Concord Originals’ Emmy-nominated exec VP Sophia Dilley (HBO’s “Stax: Soulsville, USA”) and VP of development Charles Hopkins (“Billy Preston: That’s the Way God Planned It”). Concord Originals’ Quíle Miguel Gomez, Victor Zaraya and Bob Valentine executive produce.

“We’re film executives within a music and theater company, and this is the first play we’ve commissioned and developed entirely in-house. I’m really excited about what this represents and how it demonstrates our team’s ability to go beyond simply packaging and building projects,” said Dilley.

Hopkins concurred: “This project encapsulates exactly what Concord Originals is all about: identifying world-class IP under the BMG roof and reimagining it for screen.”

The eight-person team of this relatively young division, founded in 2021, has its work cut out for it, given the deep vaults of BMG’s catalog plus those of Concord Theatricals (18,000+) and the recent acquisition of RKO, which adds another 5,000+ IP assets, including such classic cinema titles as Alfred Hitchcock’s “Suspicion” and Fritz Lang’s “The Woman in the Window.”

“RKO has such a rich history and so much of it hasn’t been oversaturated, which makes it ripe for reinvention and a chance to reach new generations. It also feels like a deep well of stories that’s particularly timely for where the industry is today. Historically, RKO was interesting in how it made films on contained budgets, and I’m excited to explore what that approach could look like today,” Dilley remarked.

Concord Originals’ scripted slate also includes “Bleeding Love,” an original movie musical featuring hits from multi Grammy-winner Ryan Tedder, a limited series adaptation of Rodgers & Hammerstein’s “Cinderella” with Skydance TV and projects with Jennifer Lopez’s Nuyorican Prods. for Disney+.

Reflecting on the fact that “Wolf” director San Martin is a protégé of BMG client Lin-Manuel Miranda for the Rolex Mentor and Protégé Arts Initiative, Hopkins said: “That is the advantage we now have in this bigger organization, is that there are these connecting dots between all of the talent that we work with on the filmmaking side as well as the music and theater side. So I think we’ll encounter more and more of those common connections among the artists we work with in the future.”

Dilley pointed out that a single property could generate a film, stage production, album and other associated content.

“We cover so many different areas, so the exciting part is bringing them together. Our ambition is to take a 360 approach to projects wherever we can, connecting film and TV with music and theater … bringing artists into a series, playwrights into the writing process, or developing a film that also has a stage production and an accompanying album,” said Dilley.

“We have the opportunity to be involved in the life cycle of these copyrights, from beginning to end. Ultimately, it’s about keeping artists, iconic legacies and great IP in the zeitgeist, championing them and giving them the biggest cultural impact possible. That’s what guides everything we do.”

Don't throw away your old phone box — you might need it in an emergency

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

That cardboard box in your closet can come in handy more often than you’d think.

There's a specific type of anxiety that comes with deciding what to do with a box. Do you immediately throw it away? Do you stash it in a closet with about 20 other boxes that store little more than your own guilt? If you chuck it, you might need it for some unknown purpose in the future. But if you don't, that box might just sit in your home indefinitely.

It doesn't help when it's a nice box, either, like the type your phone might have arrived in. It's likely made of thick cardboard, with convenient compartments. Luckily, or perhaps unluckily for those who have finally gathered the nerve to go on a recycling tear, there are a few ways to help you decide the fate of your phone's box. Whether you're ready to resell your phone or need to report a stolen one, here's why you might want to keep your phone's box.

Hopefully, you and your phone will never part ways without your consent. However, if it does get stolen, you'll need its IMEI (International Mobile Equipment Identity) number. An IMEI is a 15- 17-digit number unique to your phone. Carriers can use it to identify the device and block it if it's lost or stolen. AT&T describes it as your phone's "fingerprint."

One place to check for your IMEI number is the box your phone came in. You should also be able to find it through your Apple or Google account, depending on the device you have. Still, in the heat of the moment, grabbing that box from a drawer might be the easiest option.

Some less stressful scenarios might also lead you to keep your phone box. If you purchased an iPhone from Apple directly, for example, you have 14 days to return it from the day you received it. It must also be in the original packaging. Likewise, Samsung requires original packaging. Other retailers might have different policies, so at the very least, it's a good idea to check before you trash the box. Or keep it until after the window has closed, just to be safe.

Additionally, if you're thinking of reselling your phone at some point down the line, having its original box could come in handy. For one, you might have saved additional cables or something like a SIM ejector tool that a new owner will want. And while there's no hard data, some experts believe that having that original box can boost your odds of selling your phone at a good price.

Consumer Reports suggests the box increases the market value of the item and shows that you've taken good care of it. If you saw two identical iPhones for the same price, but only one was in a box, would you really choose the one without the box? The article also notes that you'll already have a box if you need to ship your phone.

So, if you're waffling on the eternal question of whether to save your phone's box, you can consider if any of those situations apply to you. And if not, there's likely a recycling bin somewhere waiting for its next deposit.

The coolest ride in town is England’s first electric ice cream truck

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The “world’s leading ice cream truck manufacturer” has released its first fully electric ice cream truck, which is already in operaiton, and it says it’s open for orders for more.

Whitby Morrison bills itself as “the world’s leading ice cream truck manufacturer,” supplying converted vans as ice cream trucks to drivers worldwide.

And now it has taken a big step into the modern age delivering its first fully electric ice cream truck to Hocking’s dairy in North Devon, England.

The truck has been operating since July, through the course of one “ice cream season.” It reportedly covered more than 100 miles a day and has done days serving up to 12 hours at a time.

It’s built on a standard Mercedes eSprinter, so it’s not retrofitted from the diesel vans that make up most of Whitby Morrison’s other offerings. The eSprinter comes in a “chassis cab” configuration which comes with just the chassis and cab, and lets upfitters build whatever they want onto the back end of the vehicle.

In this case, it also got some pretty extensive (and a little over-the-top) art all around the vehicle, highlighting the van’s electric operation.

The ice cream truck maker, Whitby Morrison, says this is the “world’s first fully certified electric ice cream truck.”

We’re not sure what sort of international certifications are required for an ice cream truck so we’ll take their word for it, but we have seen a Rivian EDV turned into a Ben & Jerry’s ice cream truck, complete with side window, and a concept electric ice cream truck as far back as 2019. So it’s certainly not the very first time this idea has been had, at least.

And it is a good idea. As with school buses, ice cream trucks are vehicles that children are likely to congregate around. Having a stinky old truck spewing emissions just a few feet away from those kids’ lungs is the sort of thing that can expose them to a life of health problems… and we might as well not stack that on top of the sugar addiction we’re teaching them to have (hey, at least they have to get outside to chase the truck down, so they’re getting some physical activity out of it, right?)

It also opens up more possibilities for operators to serve at events where having a diesel truck running might not be reasonable, like in enclosed spaces or where quiet might be appreciated (this came up when we went to Harbinger Motors’ factory opening, where local food trucks were in attendance, inside the large factory building, and made the air quality noticeably worse).

Whitby Morrison says it’s taking orders both in the UK and overseas, with US orders just opening. To learn more, head on over to its website.

Could Clarke be UK fighter of year after Billam-Smith KO?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cheavon Clarke upset expectations to take out both Jack Massey and former world champion Chris Billam-Smith this year; Billam-Smith had never been dropped or stopped before; Watch Carlos Adames vs Gerome Warburton from 4pm live on Sky Sports Mix on Saturday

Sports Journalist @johndennen

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Cheavon Clarke should be on the shortlist for British fighter of the year after his victories over Jack Massey and Chris Billam-Smith.

Clarke went into both of those fights perceived as the underdog and won both by stoppage.

Billam-Smith, renowned for his toughness, had never been knocked down before. He'd never even been dropped before.

But Clarke put him down twice, heavily, and hammered him to an inside-the-distance defeat.

He's in the kind of form that should make him a candidate for British boxer of the year.

"He definitely should be [on the shortlist] and hopefully next year he could maybe get a world title and be the UK fighter of the year," Dan Azeez, a former European champion, told Sky Sports.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"He had two back to back losses and then he's come back, beat Jack Massey in a stoppage, got off the canvas to win, and now he's just beat Chris Billam-Smith.

"He's just come and destroyed him in excellent fashion," Azeez added. "Those are the kind of stories that inspire people."

Britain has strong contenders for boxer of the year, not least Daniel Dubois becoming a two-time heavyweight world champion.

"It has to be Daniel Dubois, if I'm being honest," Azeez said, adding other contenders: "Willie Hutchinson WBC light-heavyweight champion… Ellie Scotney [who went undisputed].

"Chev, he could be," he continued. "He's definitely in the mix, he's done very well this year and my guy Denzel Bentley [who became world champion and beat Shane Mosley Jr]."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Former world champion George Groves told Sky Sports: "He's had a great year." Though Dubois would be his overall pick too.

"When you talk about Willie Hutchinson winning a world title this year, Daniel Dubois - two Wardley fights. Probably Dubois. To get off the floor in the first fight and look so spectacular in the second fight," he noted.

Clarke though deserves full credit for his performance against a former world champion in Billam-Smith.

"A great win for him," Groves said. "He had a plan, he stuck to it and found that shot that no one's found so far who's fought Chris Billam-Smith. It was a great, great performance from him."

It was an expert finish. "He stayed on him, didn't get reckless," Groves said. "Chris Billam-Smith's got fantastic powers of recovery, can weather a storm, can fight tired, can fight buzzed and still be effective so good for him."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

It probably a merits a fight with someone like Jai Opetaia, widely regarded as the best fighter in the division. But Opetaia has a heavyweight bout against Deontay Wilder in December and won't be available to box for some time.

What should Clarke do next? "Does that feel like a step down if he fights [Ryan] Rozicki or [Brandon] Glanton? It would be a good fight," Groves said.

"Maybe Chris Billam-Smith will campaign for a rematch but maybe that's not the best thing for him right now.

"Chev's got the world at his feet in many ways now."

Watch Carlos Adames vs Gerome Warburton from 4pm live on Sky Sports Mix on Saturday.
